By iterating the argument, we can cover the entire interval to
[0,T]
. Details are left to the reader.
∎

We are now in position to prove
Theorem

2.16
:

Theorem 4.12
.

In addition to Assumption
(
B
)
,
assume that

i.

the coefficient
(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto f_{t}^{0}(x_{0},\mu)
is Hölder continuous in time, uniformly in

(x_{0},\mu)
; the coefficient
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto f_{t}(x_{0},x,\mu)
is Hölder continuous in time,
uniformly in
(x_{0},x,\mu)
;

ii.

x_{0}\mapsto g^{0}(x_{0},\mu)
has
Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
\mu
;
x_{0}\mapsto g(x_{0},x,\mu)

has Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
(x,\mu)
.

Then,
under the same conditions (i) and (ii) as in the statement of
Theorem

4.11
,
the functions
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
are the unique solutions
to the
master equation
(
2.27
)–(
2.28
)
in the class of pairs

(V^{0},V)
such that

1.

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto(\partial_{t}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu),\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu),\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
;

(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\mapsto(\partial_{\mu}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y),\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
;

2.

(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto(\partial_{t}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla^{2}_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
;

(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\mapsto(\partial_{\mu}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y),\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,

3.

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)
and
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto(\nabla_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu))
are Lipschitz
continuous with respect to
(x_{0},\mu)
and
(x_{0},x,\mu)
respectively
(using the distance
{\mathbb{W}}_{1}
to handle the argument
\mu
), uniformly in
t
;

4.

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto(V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu),\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu))

and

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto(\|V(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\|_{\textrm{\rm\text{{r}}}},\|\nabla_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\|_{\textrm{\rm\text{{r}}}})
are globally
bounded, for any
\textrm{\rm\text{{r}}}\in[1,\lfloor\textrm{\rm\text{{s}}}\rfloor-(d/2+1)]\setminus{\mathbb{N}}
.

In particular,

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
have
first-order derivative in
t
and
second-order derivatives in
x_{0}
, which are jointly continuous with respect to all their arguments.

The proof relies on
the iteration principle introduced in the proof of Theorem

4.11
. We use in particular the same notation as therein.

On the interval
[T-\delta,T]
, the result follows from
Corollary

5.17
and Proposition
5.19
(item iv).
It says in particular that, at time
T-\delta
, the function

x_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(T-\delta,x_{0},\mu)
has
Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
\mu
, and the function
x_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}(T-\delta,x_{0},x,\mu)

has Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
(x,\mu)
. This makes it possible to reapply
Corollary

5.17

and Proposition
5.19
(item iv)
but on the interval

[T-(\delta+\delta^{\prime}),T-\delta]
. By continuity properties of

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
(and of their derivatives), we get a solution
on
[T-(\delta+\delta^{\prime}),T]
. Again,
Corollary

5.17
and Proposition
5.19
(item iv) say that the functions

x_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(T-(\delta+\delta^{\prime}),x_{0},\mu)
has
Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
\mu
, and the function
x_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}(T-(\delta+\delta^{\prime}),x_{0},x,\mu)

has Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
(x,\mu)
. By iterating, we get that
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and

{\mathcal{U}}
solve the two master equations on
the entire
[0,T]
.

We now turn to
uniqueness.
By
Proposition

2.15
, we know that any solution

(V^{0},V)
to the master equation, as given in the statement, induces a solution to the
forward-backward system
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
) (for a given initial condition
(x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
). The bounds on

(V^{0},V)
required in item (4) of the statement say that the resulting solution

({\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}^{0})

to
(
2.12
)
takes values in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\text{{r}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\textrm{\rm\text{{r}}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, for any

\textrm{\rm\text{{r}}}\in[1,\lfloor\text{{s}}\rfloor-(d/2+1)]\setminus{\mathbb{N}}
.
Then,
Lemmas

3.1

and

3.2
make it possible to gain extra regularity and guarantee
that (ii) and (iii) in item 2 of Definition

2.10

are satisfied.
This shows that, for any fixed
initial condition
(x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,

(V^{0},V)
induces a solution to (
2.11
)–(
2.12
) in
the sense of Definition

2.10
. Uniqueness
to (
2.11
)–(
2.12
), as given by
Theorem
4.11
under the initial condition

(x_{0},\mu)
, together with the representation formula
(
4.6
)
imply that

(V^{0},V)
is necessarily equal to

({\mathcal{U}}^{0},{\mathcal{U}})
.
∎

4.7
Proof of Proposition
4.4

Recall that
(({\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0}),({\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}^{0}}))
is the unique solution of (
4.4
)–(
4.5
) and has the representation (
4.6
). For any
\varepsilon>0
, let
(({\bm{X}}^{0,\varepsilon},{\bm{Y}}^{0,\varepsilon},{\bm{Z}}^{0,\varepsilon}),({\bm{\mu}}^{\varepsilon},{\bm{u}}^{\varepsilon},{\bm{v}^{0,\varepsilon}}))
be the unique solution of (
4.4
)–(
4.5
) corresponding to the initial data
(x_{0}+\varepsilon(x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}),\mu+\varepsilon(\mu^{\prime}-\mu))
. By Proposition
4.1
, we have,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

(4.32)

\begin{split}&Y_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}={\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon},\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}),\ Z_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon},\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}),\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&u_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)={\mathcal{U}}(t,X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon},x,\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}),\ v_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}(x)=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon},x,\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}),\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}.\end{split}

We let

\begin{split}&\left((\triangle{\bm{X}}^{0,\varepsilon},\triangle{\bm{Y}}^{0,\varepsilon},\triangle{\bm{Z}}^{0,\varepsilon}),(\triangle{\bm{\mu}}^{\varepsilon},\triangle{\bm{u}}^{\varepsilon},\triangle{\bm{v}^{0,\varepsilon}})\right)\\
&:=\left(\Bigl(\frac{{\bm{X}}^{0,\varepsilon}-{\bm{X}}^{0}}{\varepsilon},\frac{{\bm{Y}}^{0,\varepsilon}-{\bm{Y}}^{0}}{\varepsilon},\frac{{\bm{Z}}^{0,\varepsilon}-{\bm{Z}}^{0}}{\varepsilon}\Bigr),\Bigl(\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\varepsilon}-{\bm{\mu}}}{\varepsilon},\frac{{\bm{u}}^{\varepsilon}-{\bm{u}}}{\varepsilon},\frac{{\bm{v}^{0,\varepsilon}-\bm{v}^{0}}}{\varepsilon}\Bigr)\right).\end{split}

First Step.
Let us first solve the following forward system

(4.33)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}\delta X_{t}^{0}=-\delta\bigl[\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\bigr]\mathrm{d}t,\\
&\partial_{t}\delta\mu_{t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta\mu_{t}-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))\delta\mu_{t}\Bigr)-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\delta\bigl[\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0},\cdot,\mu_{t}))\bigr]\mu_{t}\Bigr)=0,\end{split}

for
t\in[0,T]
,
with the two initial conditions

\delta X_{0}^{0}=x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}
and

\delta\mu_{0}=\mu^{\prime}-\mu
. Above, the second equation is formally obtained from the forward equation in (
4.9
) by replacing the term
{\rm div}_{x}(\delta[\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))]\mu_{t})
by
{\rm div}_{x}(\delta[\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0},\cdot,\mu_{t}))]\mu_{t})
.
Since
\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}
is
x_{0}
-continuously differentiable with a bounded derivative
(see (
3.19
)), we easily deduce from the theory of ODEs that the first equation in (
4.33
) admits a unique solution
{\delta\bm{X}^{0}}
in the sense of item 1 in Definition
4.2
. It satisfies
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta X_{t}^{0}|\leq C_{T}|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}|
. Moreover,

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

(4.34)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\Delta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}|\leq C_{T}|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}|,\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-X_{t}^{0}\right|=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\Delta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\delta X_{t}^{0}\right|=0.

Now let us turn to the second equation in (
4.33
), which we prove to be solvable by linearizing the
forward equation in (
4.5
). To do so, we represent the solutions by means on an SDE, very much in the spirit of Lemma

2.4
.
Let
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{F}},{{\mathbb{P}}})

be a filtered probability space (different from
(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{{\mathbb{P}}}^{0})
)
equipped with an
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
-valued
{\mathbb{F}}
-Brownian
(B_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

and two
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
-valued random variables
\xi
and
\xi^{\varepsilon}

satisfying

{\mathbb{P}}\circ\xi^{-1}=\mu_{0}=\mu
,

{\mathbb{P}}\circ(\xi^{\varepsilon})^{-1}=\mu_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\mu+\varepsilon(\mu^{\prime}-\mu)
and

\mathbb{E}[|\xi^{\varepsilon}-\xi|]=\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{0})
. Let also
(X_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{0\leq t\leq T},(X_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
be the solutions to

(4.35)

\displaystyle X_{t}^{\varepsilon}=\xi^{\varepsilon}-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{p}H\left(X_{s}^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(s,X_{s}^{0,\varepsilon},X_{s}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{s}^{\varepsilon})\right)\mathrm{d}s+B_{t},\quad t\in[0,T],

(4.36)

\displaystyle X_{t}=\xi-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{p}H\left(X_{s},\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(s,X_{s}^{0},X_{s},\mu_{s})\right)\mathrm{d}s+B_{t},\quad t\in[0,T].

Then
{\mathbb{P}}\circ(X_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{-1}=\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}
and
{\mathbb{P}}\circ X_{t}^{-1}=\mu_{t}
for any
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely.
We have

\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{t}^{\varepsilon}-X_{t}|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\xi^{\varepsilon}-\xi|\right]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl|\nabla_{p}H\left(X_{s}^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(s,X_{s}^{0,\varepsilon},X_{s}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{s}^{\varepsilon})\right)-\nabla_{p}H\left(X_{s},\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(s,X_{s}^{0},X_{s},\mu_{s})\right)\Bigr|\mathrm{d}s\bigg].

Since
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to
{\mathscr{C}}(\kappa,1,2)
for every
t\in[0,T]
and for some

\kappa>0
, the gradient
\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}
is Lipschitz in the variable
(x_{0},x,\mu)
(with

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
being equipped with

{\mathbb{W}}_{1}
), uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
.
By (
4.34
) and Gronwall’s lemma, we deduce that,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

(4.37)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{t})=0.

In fact, we claim that the result also holds in total variation.
When
\mu_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\mu
(i.e.,
\mu^{\prime}=\mu
),
this follows from

[
42
, (3.1) and Proof of Theorem 2.4]

(which rely on Pinsker’s inequality and Girsanov theorem)
together with
(
4.34
)
and
(
4.37
).
When
\mu^{\prime}\not=\mu
, one must introduce
the solution
\hat{\bm{X}}^{\varepsilon}

to the SDE
(
4.35
) when initialized from

\xi
at time
0
.
Then,

[
42
, (3.1) and Proof of Theorem 2.4]

say that,

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

(4.38)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d_{\rm TV}\Bigl({\mathbb{P}}\circ(\hat{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{-1},\mu_{t}\Bigr)=0.

Also, since
d_{\rm TV}(\mu_{0}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{0})=d_{\rm TV}((1-\varepsilon)\mu+\varepsilon\mu^{\prime},\mu)\leq 4\varepsilon
, we can now choose

\xi^{\varepsilon}
in
(
4.35
) such that
{\mathbb{P}}(\{\xi=\xi^{\varepsilon}\})\geq 1-4\varepsilon
. Then, it is easy to see that

{\mathbb{P}}(\{\hat{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}=X_{t}^{\varepsilon}\})\geq 1-4\varepsilon
, for all

t\in[0,T]
. Since
{\mathbb{P}}\circ(\xi^{\varepsilon})^{-1}
is still required to be equal to

\mu_{0}^{\varepsilon}
, this does not change the flow

({\mathbb{P}}\circ(X_{t}^{\varepsilon})^{-1})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,
which is still equal to

(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{0\leq t\leq T}
. We deduce that,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

(4.39)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d_{\rm TV}(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon},\mu_{t})=0.

We note that
\triangle{\bm{\mu}}^{\varepsilon}=(\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
satisfies
(in a weak sense similar to
(
4.10
))

(4.40)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\left(\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\right)-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\left(\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\right)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\rm div}_{x}\bigl(\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\bigr)-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}\Bigl[\Gamma_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}(\cdot)\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+\Big(\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Bigr]\Bigr)=0,\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&\Gamma_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}(x):=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{pp}^{2}H\Bigl(x,\theta\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon},x,\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon})+(1-\theta)\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{xx_{0}}^{2}\mathcal{U}\Bigl(t,\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},x,\theta\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)\mu_{t}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta\right),\\
&\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}(x,y):=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{pp}^{2}H\Bigl(x,\theta\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon},x,\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon})+(1-\theta)\nabla_{x}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\Bigr)d\theta\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\cdot\left(\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x}\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{U}\Bigl(t,\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},x,\theta\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)\mu_{t},y\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta\right),\end{split}

the dots right above being understood as matricial products and
the duality bracket between

\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon}
and

\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot)
in
(
4.40
) being acting on the variable
y
of the latter.
Following the proof of Lemma
4.8
and using the fact that
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{C}}(\kappa,1,2)
, we get,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\|\triangle\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}\|_{-1}&\leq C\biggl[\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{-1}+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl[\bigl\|\Gamma_{s}^{1,\varepsilon}(\cdot)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl|\triangle X_{s}^{0,\varepsilon}\bigr|+\Bigl\|\Big(\Gamma_{s}^{2,\varepsilon}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle{\mu}_{s}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\Bigr]\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\leq C\biggl[\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{-1}+\int_{0}^{t}\Bigl(|\Delta X_{s}^{0,\varepsilon}|+\|\Delta\mu_{s}^{\varepsilon}\|_{-1}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr].\end{split}

By
(
4.34
)
and
Gronwall’s lemma, we obtain, with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,

(4.41)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\triangle\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}\|_{-1}\leq C\biggl[|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}|+\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{-1}\biggr].

Now, for any sequence of reals
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k\geq 1}
converging to
0
, we let

\begin{split}&\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}:=\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}-\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k}},\quad\triangle^{2}{X}_{t}^{0,k^{\prime},k}:=\triangle{X}_{t}^{0,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}-\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon_{k}},\\
&\triangle{\Gamma_{t}^{1,k^{\prime},k}}:=\Gamma_{t}^{1,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}-\Gamma_{t}^{1,\varepsilon_{k}},\,\,\,\quad\triangle{\Gamma_{t}^{2,k^{\prime},k}}:=\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}-\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon_{k}},\quad t\in[0,T].\end{split}

Then, we can verify that
\triangle^{2}{\bm{\mu}}^{k^{\prime},k}=(\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{t}^{k^{\prime},k})_{0\leq t\leq T}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}\right)-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\left(\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}\right)-{\rm div}_{x}\left(\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k}}\Bigl[\Gamma_{t}^{1,\varepsilon_{k}}(\cdot)\triangle^{2}X_{t}^{0,k^{\prime},k}+\Big(\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon_{k}}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}\Big)\Bigr]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k}}\Bigl[\triangle\Gamma_{t}^{1,k^{\prime},k}(\cdot)\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}+\Big(\triangle\Gamma_{t}^{2,k^{\prime},k}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}\Big)\Bigr]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\bigl(\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}-\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k}}\bigr)\Bigl[\Gamma_{t}^{1,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}(\cdot)\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}+\Big(\Gamma_{t}^{2,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle{\mu}_{t}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}\Big)\Bigr]\Bigr)=0,\quad t\in[0,T].

Again, following the proof of Lemma
4.8
, we can show that,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,
for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\|\triangle^{2}\mu_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}\|_{-1}&\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\biggl[\bigl\|\Gamma_{s}^{1,\varepsilon_{k}}(\cdot)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl|\triangle^{2}X_{t}^{0,k^{\prime},k}\bigr|+\bigl\|\triangle\Gamma_{s}^{1,k^{\prime},k}(\cdot)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl|\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}\bigr|\\
&\hskip 30.0pt+\Bigl\|\Big(\Gamma_{s}^{2,\varepsilon_{k}}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle^{2}{\mu}_{s}^{k^{\prime},k}\Big)\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}+\Bigl\|\Big(\triangle\Gamma_{s}^{2,k^{\prime},k}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle{\mu}_{s}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}\Big)\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt+{d}_{\rm TV}\bigl(\mu_{s}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}},\mu_{s}^{\varepsilon_{k}}\bigr)\Bigl(\bigl\|\Gamma_{s}^{1,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}(\cdot)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigl|\triangle X_{s}^{0,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}\bigr|+\Bigl\|\Bigl(\Gamma_{s}^{2,\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}(\cdot,\cdot),\triangle\mu_{s}^{\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\biggr]\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Using the fact that

{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to
{\mathscr{D}}(\kappa,1,2)
for all

t\in[0,T]
, we deduce from
(
4.34
)
and
(
4.37
)
that,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{k,k^{\prime}\geq n}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\Bigl(\bigl\|\triangle\Gamma_{s}^{1,k^{\prime},k}(\cdot)\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl\|\triangle\Gamma_{s}^{2,k^{\prime},k}(x,\cdot)\bigr\|_{1}\Bigr)=0.

Moreover, by (
4.34
) again,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{k,k^{\prime}\geq n}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\bigl|\triangle^{2}X_{s}^{0,k^{\prime},k}\bigr|=0.

By
(
4.39
)
and
(
4.41
), we deduce that,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely, there exists a positive sequence
(\delta_{n})_{n\geq 1}
,
converging to
0
, such that, for all
k,k^{\prime}\geq n

and for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}\|\triangle^{2}\mu_{t}^{k^{\prime},k}\|_{-1}&\leq\delta_{n}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\triangle^{2}\mu_{s}^{k^{\prime},k}\|_{-1}\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

And then, by Gronwall’s lemma,

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

(\triangle{\bm{\mu}}^{\varepsilon_{k}})_{k\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
{\mathcal{C}}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
. Therefore, there exists a
continuous

\mathbb{F}^{0}
-progressively measurable process
\delta{\bm{\mu}}=(\delta\mu_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
with values in
\mathcal{C}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
such that,

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigl\|\triangle\mu_{t}^{k}-\delta\mu_{t}\bigr\|_{-1}=0.

Passing to the limit in
(
4.40
), we deduce that

\delta{\bm{\mu}}
satisfies
(in the same sense as (
4.10
))

(4.42)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\left(\delta\mu_{t}\right)-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\left(\delta\mu_{t}\right)-{\rm div}_{x}\bigl(\nabla_{p}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))\delta\mu_{t}\bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\mu_{t}\Bigl[\nabla_{pp}^{2}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))\nabla^{2}_{xx_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\delta X_{t}^{0}+\Big(\nabla_{pp}^{2}H(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(\cdot))\nabla_{x}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot),\delta{\mu}_{t}\Big)\Bigr]\Bigr)\\
&=0,\quad t\in[0,T].\end{split}

Moreover, passing to the limit in
(
4.41
), we obtain

(4.43)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-1}\leq C\biggl[|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}|+\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{-1}\biggr].

This proves that
\delta{\bm{\mu}}
solves the second equation in (
4.33
) in the sense of item 2 in Definition
4.2
.

Second Step.
We now address the linearized backward equations

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\delta Y_{t}^{0}=-\delta\bigl[f_{t}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\bigr]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\Bigl[-\bigl(Z_{t}^{0}-\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0})\bigr)\cdot\delta\bigl[\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\bigr]-\delta\bigl[L^{0}(X_{t}^{0},-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\bigr]\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\bigl(\nabla_{x_{0}}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0})-\nabla_{x_{0}}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\bigr)\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}\mathrm{d}t

(4.44)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\bigl(\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0})-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\bigr)\cdot\delta Z_{t}^{0}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\sigma_{0}\delta Z_{t}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T],

\displaystyle\delta Y_{T}^{0}=\delta\bigl[g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T})\bigr],

and

(4.45)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}_{t}\delta u_{t}(x)=\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta u_{t}(x)+\nabla_{p}H(x,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x))\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta u_{t}(x)-\delta\bigl[f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\bigr]\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t,\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\Bigl(\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0})-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\Bigr)\cdot\delta v_{t}^{0}(x)\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\delta\Bigl[\Bigl(\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0})-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\Bigr)\Bigr]\cdot v_{t}^{0}(x)\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sigma_{0}\delta v_{t}^{0}(x)\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\\
&\delta u_{T}(x)=\delta\bigl[g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})\bigr],\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}.\end{split}

We first address
(
4.44
).
Recalling that
Z_{t}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}\mathcal{U}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})
is bounded, it follows from the standard BSDE theory that (
4.44
) admits a solution
({\delta\bm{Y}}^{0},{\delta\bm{Z}}^{0})\in{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R})\times{\mathscr{H}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Moreover,
following

[
54
]
,
we have

(4.46)

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\triangle Y_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\delta Y_{t}^{0}|^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|\triangle Z_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\delta Z_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]=0,\end{split}

which, together with the first step and the fact that

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(\kappa,1)
for all

t\in[0,T]
, implies

\delta Y_{t}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}+\left(\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{U}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t},\cdot),\delta\mu_{t}\right),\quad t\in[0,T].

Because
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}(\kappa,1)
,
for all
t\in[0,T]
, and
|{\delta\bm{X}^{0}}|
and
\|\delta\bm{\mu}\|_{-1}
are bounded, we have

\Bigl\|\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\delta Y_{t}^{0}|\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},\mathbb{P}^{0})}<\infty.

Then we can follow the proof of Lemma
3.8
to deduce that

\sup_{\tau}\Big\|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Big[\int_{[\tau,T]}|\delta Z_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t|{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau}^{0}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})}<\infty,

where the supremum is taken over all stopping times
\tau
. This justifies that
({\delta\bm{Y}}^{0},{\delta\bm{Z}}^{0})
solves
(
4.44
)
in the sense of item 1 in Definition
4.2
.

The well-posedness of the equation (
4.45
) is obtained via
[
30
, Theorem 2.2]
, with solutions
in the set of
progressively-measurable processes

(\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{v}}^{0})
with values in
(the separable space)

{\mathcal{H}}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times[{\mathcal{H}}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})]^{d}
,
such that
\delta{\bm{u}}
has continuous trajectories and
for which the following bound holds true:

\mathbb{E}^{0}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\delta u_{t}\|_{1,2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\delta v^{0}_{t}\|_{1,2}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

Note that

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}_{t}\left(\triangle u^{\varepsilon}_{t}\right)=\Bigl[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\left(\triangle u^{\varepsilon}_{t}\right)+\Upsilon_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)\cdot\nabla_{x}\triangle u^{\varepsilon}_{t}-\nabla_{x}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)\cdot\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\left(\delta_{\mu}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x,\cdot),\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}_{t}\right)\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\Bigl(\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0})-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0}))\Bigr)\cdot\triangle v_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\Bigl[(j_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}-j_{t}^{3,\varepsilon})\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+j_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}\triangle Z_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}\Bigr]\cdot v_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}(x)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\triangle v_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\\
&\triangle u_{T}^{\varepsilon}(x)=\nabla_{x}g^{\varepsilon}(x)\cdot\triangle X_{T}^{0,\varepsilon}+\left(\delta_{\mu}g^{\varepsilon}(x,\cdot),\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}_{T}\right),\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&\Upsilon_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x):=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{p}H\bigl(x,\theta\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)+(1-\theta)\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta,\\
&\nabla_{x}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x):=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x}f_{t}\bigl(\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},x,\theta\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)\mu_{t}\bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta,\quad\text{and similarly for }\nabla_{x}g^{\varepsilon}(x)\\
&\delta_{\mu}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x,y):=\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{\mu}f_{t}\bigl(\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},x,\theta\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)\mu_{t},y\bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta,\quad\text{and similarly for }\partial_{\mu}g^{\varepsilon}(x,y)\\
&j_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}:=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x_{0}p}^{2}H^{0}\bigl(\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},\theta Z_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)Z_{t}^{0}\bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta,\\
&j_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}:=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{pp}^{2}H^{0}\bigl(\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},\theta Z_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)Z_{t}^{0}\bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta,\\
&j_{t}^{3,\varepsilon}:=\int_{0}^{1}[\nabla_{x_{0}p}^{2}H^{0}+\nabla_{pp}^{2}H^{0}\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}x_{0}}w^{0}]\bigl(\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}w^{0}(t,\theta X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+(1-\theta)X_{t}^{0})\bigr)\mathrm{d}\theta.\end{split}

Using the first step together with the representation formulas
(
4.32
), there is no difficulty in proving
that

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl[\bigl\|\bigl(\Upsilon_{t}^{\varepsilon}-\Upsilon_{t}^{0}\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{x}\triangle u_{t}^{\varepsilon}\bigr\|_{0}^{2}\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t=0,\\
&\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl[\bigl\|\nabla_{x}f_{t}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},\cdot,\mu_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}\bigr\|_{0}^{2}\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t=0,\\
&\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl[\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl|\bigl(\delta_{\mu}f_{t}^{\varepsilon},\triangle\mu_{t}^{\varepsilon}\bigr)-\bigl(\nabla_{\mu}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t},\cdot),\delta\mu_{t}\bigr)\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t=0,\\
&\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl\|\Bigl[\bigl(j_{t}^{1,\varepsilon}-j_{t}^{3,\varepsilon}\bigr)\triangle X_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}+j_{t}^{2,\varepsilon}\triangle Z_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}\Bigr]\cdot v_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\Bigl[\bigl(j_{t}^{1,0}-j_{t}^{3,0}\bigr)\delta X_{t}^{0}+j_{t}^{2,0}\delta Z_{t}^{0}\Bigr]\cdot v_{t}^{0}\Bigr\|_{0}^{2}\mathrm{d}t=0.\end{split}

Proceeding in the same manner for the terminal condition, we can invoke
[
30
, Theorem 2.2]
again to obtain

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\mathbb{E}^{0}\left[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\triangle u_{t}^{\varepsilon}-\delta u_{t}\|_{1,2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\triangle v_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}-\delta v^{0}_{t}\|_{1,2}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right]=0.

Since
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}(\kappa,1,3+\alpha)
for all

t\in[0,T]
, we can derive that

\delta u_{t}(x)=\nabla_{x_{0}}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}+\left(\delta_{\mu}\mathcal{U}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t},\cdot),\delta\mu_{t}\right),

for any
t\in[0,T]
with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,
which implies that
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\delta u_{t}\|_{3+\alpha}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})
.
Further applying (
4.11
), we can verify that
{\delta\bm{u}}
has continuous trajectories in
{\mathcal{C}}^{3+\alpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Inserting the above identity in
(
4.42
), we obtain
(
4.9
).

Moreover,
from the representation formula
(
4.32
)
together with
(
4.34
)
and
(
4.41
), and the fact that
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to

{\mathscr{D}}(\kappa,1,3+\alpha)
, we deduce that

\|\triangle v^{0,\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{3+\alpha}\leq C\biggl[|x_{0}^{\prime}-x_{0}|+\|\mu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{-1}\biggr],

which proves by passing to the limit that

\delta v_{t}^{0}
takes values in a compact subset of

{\mathcal{C}}^{3+\epsilon}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\alpha)
.
Therefore,
\delta{\bm{v}}^{0}
, regarded as a process taking values in

{\mathcal{C}}^{3+\epsilon}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, is progressively-measurable (in the

Bochner
sense).

In the end, we obtain
({\delta\bm{u}},{\delta\bm{v}}^{0})
solves the second equation of the backward system (
4.9
) in the sense of item 2 in Definition
4.2
.
We finally get
(({\delta\bm{X}}^{0},{\delta\bm{Y}}^{0},{\delta\bm{Z}}^{0}),

({\delta\bm{\mu}},{\delta\bm{u}},{\delta\bm{v}}^{0}))

is a solution to the system
(
4.8
)–(
4.9
) in the sense of
Definition

4.2
.
∎

5
Short time analysis

In this section we study the local well-posedness of the
forward-backward system
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
)
(and then of the
master equations (
2.27
)-(
2.28
)).
One of the difficulties to do so is that the coefficients of
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
) are not Lipschitz continuous (due to the quadratic growth of the two Lagrangians
L^{0}
and
L
in the control variable
\alpha
). Conceptually, this does not cause any major trouble because
we know in the end (from the analysis carried out in the previous sections, see in particular (
4.23
)) that

|Z_{t}^{0}|
and

|\nabla_{x}u_{t}|
are bounded
(by deterministic constants).
However, these bounds cannot be directly used in the small time analysis.

To circumvent this difficulty, we introduce two auxiliary functions
\hat{L}^{0}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
and

\hat{H}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
that are globally Lipschitz continuous.
The function
(x_{0},p)\mapsto\hat{L}^{0}(x_{0},p)
is understood as an approximation of the function

(x_{0},p)\mapsto L^{0}(x_{0},-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(x_{0},p))
and the function
(x,p)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\mapsto\hat{H}(x,p)
is understood as an approximation of
H
.

In a first time, we thus focus on the systems of two forward-backward equations (set on
an arbitrary
filtered probability space

(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})

satisfying the usual conditions and
equipped with a
Brownian motion
(B_{t}^{0})_{0\leq t\leq T}
with values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
):

(5.1)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{0}=-\nabla_{p}H^{0}\bigl(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&\mathrm{d}Y_{t}^{0}=-\Bigl(f_{t}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0}\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}Z_{t}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&X_{0}^{0}=x_{0},\quad Y_{T}^{0}=g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T}),\end{split}

and

(5.2)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\mu_{t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\mu_{t}-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{t}\bigr)\mu_{t}\Bigr)=0,\quad\ (t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\\
&\mathrm{d}_{t}u_{t}(x)=\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}u_{t}(x)+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x)\bigr)-f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}m_{t}(x),\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\\
&\mu_{0}=\mu,\quad u_{T}(x)=g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T}),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}.\end{split}

This is only in the end of the section that we return back to the original
equations (
2.11
)
and
(
2.12
), see Subsection
5.8
.
Notice in particular that, differently from
(
2.12
), the martingale part in the backward equation of
(
5.2
)
is not represented as
a stochastic integral (as there is no need at this stage).

We thus introduce the following variant of Assumption (
A
):

Assumption Â
.
There exist four reals
{\mathfrak{L}}>0
,

\kappa>0
,

\hat{\kappa}>0
and
{\mathbb{s}}>3
(
{\mathbb{s}}\not\in{\mathbb{N}}
) such that

(Â1)

The function
(f^{0}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

satisfies

{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(\kappa,\mathbb{s})

and
the function
(f_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

satisfies

{\mathscr{D}}(\kappa,{\mathbb{s}},{\mathbb{s}})
.

(Â2)
The function
g^{0}

satisfies

{\mathscr{C}}^{0}({\mathfrak{L}},\mathbb{s})

and

{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(\hat{\kappa},\mathbb{s})
,
and
the function
g

satisfies

{\mathscr{C}}({\mathfrak{L}},{\mathbb{s}},{\mathbb{s}})

and

{\mathscr{D}}(\hat{\kappa},{\mathbb{s}},{\mathbb{s}})
.

(Â3)

The functions

H^{0}
and

\hat{L}^{0}
have continuous joint derivatives up to the order 2 in
x_{0}
and
\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor+1
in

p
, and
the function

\hat{H}
has continuous joint derivatives up to the order
\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor+1
in

(x,p)
.
All the existing derivatives (of order greater than 1) of
\hat{L}^{0}
and

\hat{H}
are bounded by
\kappa

and the quantity
\hat{L}^{0}(x_{0},p)
is bounded by

\kappa(1+|p|)
.
The quantity
H^{0}(x_{0},p)
is bounded by

\kappa(1+|p|^{2})
, the gradient
\nabla_{p}H^{0}(x_{0},p)

is bounded by
\kappa(1+|p|)
.
All the other existing derivatives of
H^{0}
are bounded by
\kappa
.

(Â4)

The function
\hat{H}

satisfies the coercivity condition

\nabla^{2}_{pp}\hat{H}(x,p)>0
(in the sense of positive definite matrices), for all
(x,p)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
For any
r>0
, we let

\zeta(r):=\inf_{(x,p)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:|p|\leq r}\inf_{\rho\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:|\rho|=1}\rho\cdot\bigl(\nabla^{2}_{pp}\hat{H}(x,p)\rho\bigr)>0.

5.1
Local well-posedness

Theorem 5.1
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
,
there exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}

and
{\mathbb{s}}

such that for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
, the forward-backward system (
5.1
)-(
5.2
) admits a unique solution
({\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0},{\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{m}})
in the sense of Definition

2.10
, i.e.,
({\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0})

is required to be
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-progressively measurable, with the trajectories of

{\bm{X}}^{0}
and
{\bm{Y}}^{0}

being continuous, such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|X_{t}^{0}|\in L^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})

and

(5.3)

\Big\|\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}^{0}|\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},\mathbb{P}^{0})}+\sup_{\tau}\bigg\|\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\int_{\tau}^{T}|Z_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\,|\,\mathcal{F_{\tau}}^{0}\bigg]\bigg\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},\mathbb{P}^{0})}<\infty,

the second supremum being taken over
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-stopping times
\tau
;
and
({\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{m}})

is required to
be an
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-progressively measurable process
with values in
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{s}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, with continuous trajectories in

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{r}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{r}}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

for any
\mathbb{r}<\mathbb{s}
,
such that

(5.4)

\Big\|\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl[\|u_{t}\|_{\mathbb{s}}\Bigr]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},\mathbb{P}^{0})}+\Big\|\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl[\|m_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}\Bigr]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},\mathbb{P}^{0})}<\infty,

and, for any
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,
(m_{t}(x))_{0\leq t\leq T}
is an
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-martingale satisfying

m_{0}(x)\equiv 0
.

Moreover, for any pair of initial conditions
((x_{0}^{i},\mu^{i}))_{i=1,2}\in[\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})]^{2}
,
the corresponding pair of solutions

(({\bm{X}}^{0,i},{\bm{Y}}^{0,i},{\bm{Z}}^{0,i},{\bm{\mu}}^{i},{\bm{u}}^{i},{\bm{m}}^{i}))_{i=1,2}
to (
5.1
)-(
5.2
) satisfy, for any
p\in[1,8]
,

(5.5)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big[\|u^{1}_{t}-u_{t}^{2}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}+\|m^{1}_{t}-m_{t}^{2}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2p}\bigg]\\
&+\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl[|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}+|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}\Bigr]+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}|Z^{0,1}_{t}-Z_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{p}\bigg]\\
&\leq C\Big[|x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{2}|^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}\Big],\end{split}

for a constant
C
only depending on

d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
and
{\mathbb{s}}
. For the same
C
, we can assume that the left-hand sides of (
5.3
)
and
(
5.4
) are bounded by
C
.

The proof relies on a suitable application of the Banach fixed point theorem.
Throughout,
T
is less than 1.

First Step.

Assuming that
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
is generated by
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}
and
{\bm{B}}^{0}
,
we first construct the mapping to which we will eventually apply the fixed point theorem.
Inputs
{\bm{X}}^{0}
are taken in the set

{\mathscr{S}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
of
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-progressively measurable
continuous processes with values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0}|\in L^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})
.
For
X^{0}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we can follow the proof of
[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1]
to obtain, for a small time horizon, a unique solution to

(5.6)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\tilde{\mu}_{t}-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(\cdot,\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}_{t})\tilde{\mu}_{t}\Bigr)=0,\\
&\mathrm{d}_{t}\tilde{u}_{t}(x)=\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\tilde{u}_{t}(x)+\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}_{t}(x))-f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\tilde{\mu}_{t})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}m_{t}(x),\quad(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\\
&\tilde{\mu}_{0}=\mu,\quad\tilde{u}_{T}(x)=g(X_{T}^{0},x,\tilde{\mu}_{T}),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\end{split}

where
(\tilde{\bm{\mu}},\tilde{\bm{u}},\tilde{\bm{m}})

is an
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-progressively measurable processes
with values in
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{s}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, with continuous trajectories in

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{r}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{r}}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

for any
\mathbb{r}<\mathbb{s}
,
such that

(5.7)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\{\|\tilde{u}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{s}}+\|m_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}\right\}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega^{0},\mathcal{F}^{0},\mathbb{P}^{0}),

and
(m_{t}(x))_{0\leq t\leq T}
is an
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-martingale starting from
0
for any
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
.

Actually, the framework is not (exactly) the same as in

[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1]
. First,
{\bm{X}}^{0}
plays the role of the common noise, even though it is not acting
additively (as
{\bm{B}}^{0}
does in
[
14
]
).
Second (and this is the main difference),

[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1]

is proven under the Lasry-Lions monotonicity condition, which we have not
assumed
here.
However, the proof
of
[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1]
(which relies on
the method of continuation) remains
conceptually the same in our context. Essentially, the parameter

\varpi
in

[
14
, Subsection 4.3.5]

is here taken as
0
and the role of
\epsilon

(which is assumed to be small in

[
14
]
)
is now played by
T
.
Although the correspondence between

\epsilon
and
T
is not immediate, the similarity between the two parameters
can be well noticed
in the proof of

[
14
, Lemma 4.3.9]
: the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that permits to pass from

[
14
, (4.30)]

to

[
14
, (4.31)]

makes an additional
\sqrt{T}
appear in front of
the parameter

\vartheta
in

[
14
, (4.30)]
.
This additional
\sqrt{T}
then plays the role of

\epsilon

in the second step of

[
14
, Subsection 4.3.5]
.
With this analogy in mind, one notices from

(c)
in the
second step of

[
14
, Subsection 4.3.5]

that the threshold
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
below which

T
must be taken only depends on

g
through
{\mathfrak{L}}
(and not
\hat{\kappa}
).

To be complete, one mentions
another subtlety: in
[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1]
,
continuities of

\tilde{\bm{u}}

and

\tilde{\bm{m}}
are stated in

{\mathcal{C}}^{\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

and

{\mathcal{C}}^{\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

respectively, but there is no difficulty in replacing
\lfloor\mathbb{s}\rfloor

by any
\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,\mathbb{s})

(for instance by combining the above bound with an interpolation argument).
In this regard, we emphasize that
Bochner
measurability
(with values in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

and

{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
) can be obtained as
explained in
Remark

2.11
, by noticing that, for
t<T
,
u_{t}
and
m_{t}
takes
values in

{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

and

{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

for some
{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}>{\mathbb{s}}
. This follows from the smoothing properties of the heat kernel:
we refer to
[
14
, (4.16)]

for the way it can be used here.
Following Proposition
3.4
(and
[
14
, Proposition 4.3.8]
), we notice that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\{\|\tilde{u}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{s}}+\|m_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}\}

can be bounded by constants only depending on the parameters

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
,
{\mathbb{s}}
and
T

(but not

\hat{\kappa}
) in (
Â
).

Next we define
(\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0},\tilde{\bm{Y}}^{0},\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{0})
as the strong solution to the following FBSDE system
(see
[
26
]
):

(5.8)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}=-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0})\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&\mathrm{d}\tilde{Y}_{t}^{0}=-\Bigl(f_{t}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},\tilde{\mu}_{t})+\hat{L}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&\tilde{X}_{0}^{0}=x_{0},\quad\tilde{Y}_{T}^{0}=g^{0}(\tilde{X}_{T}^{0},\tilde{\mu}_{T}).\end{split}

Setting
\mathfrak{T}({\bm{X}}^{0}):=\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0}
, we define a map
\mathfrak{T}:{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
In the rest of the proof, we show that
\mathfrak{T}
is a contraction mapping from
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
into itself (for
T
small enough).

Second Step.
Denote the solutions to (
5.6
) with two different inputs
{\bm{X}}^{0,1}
and
{\bm{X}}^{0,2}
with
X^{0,1}_{0}=X^{0,2}_{0}=x_{0}
by
(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{1},\tilde{\bm{u}}^{1},\tilde{\bm{m}}^{1})
and
(\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{2},\tilde{\bm{u}}^{2},\tilde{\bm{m}}^{2})
, and the corresponding solutions to (
5.8
) by
(\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0,1},\tilde{\bm{Y}}^{0,1},\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{0,1})
and
(\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0,2},\tilde{\bm{Y}}^{0,2},\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{0,2})
.
By the standard duality method of mean field games (without using the monotonicity condition of
f_{t}
and
g
, see for instance

[
14
, (4.29)]
) and thanks
to (
5.7
) (which, together with
(Â4)
, says that we are working on a subdomain of the space on which the Hessian matrix

\nabla^{2}_{pp}H
is bounded from below by a strictly positive constant), we have

(5.9)

\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(|\nabla_{x}(\tilde{u}_{t}^{1}-\tilde{u}_{t}^{2})|^{2},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1}+\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\leq C\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,1}|^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2})^{2}\biggr].

As the maps
x\mapsto\nabla_{p}H(x,\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}^{1}(t,x))
and
p\mapsto\nabla_{p}H(x,p)
are Lipschitz continuous (with Lipschitz constants only depending on the parameters

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
,
{\mathbb{s}}
and
T
,
but not

\hat{\kappa}
), we use
(
5.7
)
and
(
5.9
) to derive
(the reader may compare with the derivation of
[
14
, (3.7)]
)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl[\mathbb{W}_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2})^{2}\Bigr]\biggr]&\leq C\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(|\nabla_{x}(\tilde{u}_{t}^{1}-\tilde{u}_{t}^{2})|^{2},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1}+\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{2}\bigg]\\
&\leq CT\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(|\nabla_{x}(\tilde{u}_{t}^{1}-\tilde{u}_{t}^{2})|^{2},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1}+\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\\
&\leq CT\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,1}|^{2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2})^{2}\biggr],\end{split}

where
C

only depends on

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
,
{\mathbb{s}}
.
And then, for
CT\leq 1/2
, we obtain

(5.10)

\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl[\mathbb{W}_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2})^{2}\Bigr]\biggr]\leq 2CT\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,1}|^{2}\biggr].

By standard short time FBSDE estimates (see
[
26
]
), we can derive, still under the condition
CT\leq 1/2

(but for a possibly new value of
C
that only depends on

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
and
{\mathbb{s}}
),

(5.11)

\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl[|\tilde{X}_{t}^{0,1}-\tilde{X}_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\biggr]\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}^{0}\Bigl[\mathbb{W}_{1}(\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1},\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2})^{2}\Bigr]\leq CT\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,1}|^{2}\biggr].

Since
CT\leq 1/2
, we get

\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\tilde{X}_{t}^{0,1}-\tilde{X}_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\biggr]\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\biggr],

which implies
\mathfrak{T}
is a contraction mapping from
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Therefore, there exists a unique fixed point
{\bm{X}}^{0}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
X_{0}^{0}=x_{0}
. For this choice of
{\bm{X}}^{0}
, the FBSPDE system
(
5.6
) admits a unique solution, which we denote
({\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{m}})
.
Since
{\bm{X}}^{0}
is the fixed point of
\mathfrak{T}
, the FBSDE system (
5.1
) admits a unique strong solution
({\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0})
. In this way, we get existence and uniqueness of a solution to the pair
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
).
The bound
(
5.4
)
follows from (
5.7
)
and
(
5.3
)
follows from the argument used in the proof
of Lemma
3.8
.

Third Step.
We now prove the stability property, removing the assumption that

{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
is generated by

{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}
and
{\bm{B}}^{0}

(which will imply in particular uniqueness of the solution constructed above, but on the larger set-up

(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})
).
For any initial conditions
(x_{0}^{1},\mu^{1}),(x_{0}^{2},\mu^{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, let
({\bm{X}}^{0,1},{\bm{Y}}^{0,1},{\bm{Z}}^{0,1},{\bm{\mu}}^{1},{\bm{u}}^{1},{\bm{m}}^{1})
and
({\bm{X}}^{0,2},{\bm{Y}}^{0,2},{\bm{Z}}^{0,2},{\bm{\mu}}^{2},{\bm{u}}^{2},{\bm{m}}^{2})
be the solutions of the forward-backward system (
5.1
)-(
5.2
) with (respective) initial data
(x_{0}^{1},\mu^{1})
and
(x_{0}^{2},\mu^{2})
.

Note that

(5.12)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}_{t}\Big(u_{t}^{1}(x)-u_{t}^{2}(x)\Big)\\
&=\bigg[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\bigl(u_{t}^{1}(x)-u_{t}^{2}(x)\bigr)-\bigl(f_{t}(X_{t}^{0,1},x,\mu_{t}^{1})-f_{t}(X_{t}^{0,2},x,\mu_{t}^{2})\bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\biggl\{\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(x,r\nabla_{x}u^{1}_{t}(x)+(1-r)\nabla_{x}u^{2}(x)\bigr)\mathrm{d}r\biggr\}\cdot\nabla_{x}\bigl(u^{1}_{t}(x)-u^{2}_{t}(x)\bigr)\bigg]\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}\bigl(m_{t}^{1}(x)-m_{t}^{2}(x)\bigr).\end{split}

and

u_{T}^{1}(x)-u_{T}^{2}(x)=g(X_{T}^{0,1},x,\mu_{T}^{1})-g(X_{T}^{0,2},x,\mu_{T}^{2}).

Following
[
14
, (4.14)–(4.16)]
and recalling
T\leq 1
, we have, for
all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|u_{t}^{1}-u_{t}^{2}\|_{\mathbb{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\bigl\|f_{r}(X_{r}^{0,1},\cdot,\mu_{r}^{1})-f_{r}(X_{r}^{0,2},\cdot,\mu_{r}^{2})\bigr\|_{\mathbb{s}-1}+\bigl\|g(X_{T}^{0,1},\cdot,\mu_{T}^{1})-g(X_{T}^{0,2},\cdot,\mu_{T}^{2})\bigr\|_{\mathbb{s}}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,T]}|X_{r}^{0,1}-X_{r}^{0,2}|+\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{r}^{1},\mu_{r}^{2})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\Big],

for a constant
C
that only depends on

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
and
{\mathbb{s}}
.

Taking
power
2p
, for
p\in[1,8]
,
and then expectation (under
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
) on both sides, we obtain,
by means of Doob’s inequality,

(5.13)

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u_{t}^{1}-u_{t}^{2}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}\Bigr]\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{r\in[0,T]}|X_{r}^{0,1}-X_{r}^{0,2}|^{2p}+\sup_{r\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{r}^{1},\mu_{r}^{2})^{2p}\Big],

where we allowed the constant
C
to vary from line to line
as long as it only depends on

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
,
{\mathbb{s}}
.

Moreover,
similar to the derivation of (
5.10
) (but with a power
2p
in the computations, which does not change the argument), we can obtain
(from (
5.13
))

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2p}\Big]&\leq C\bigg\{\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}+\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|\nabla_{x}(u_{t}^{1}-u_{t}^{2})|^{2},\mu_{t}^{1}+\mu_{t}^{2}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{2p}\biggr]\biggr\}\\
&\leq C\bigg\{\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+T^{2p}\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigl\|\nabla_{x}(u^{1}_{t}-u^{2}_{t})\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2p}\Bigr]\biggr\}\\
&\leq C\bigg\{\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})+T^{2p}\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2p}\Big]\bigg\}.\end{split}

For
CT\leq 1/2
(and since
T\leq 1
), we obtain

(5.14)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2p}\Big]\leq 2C\bigg\{\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}+T\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}\Big]\bigg\}.\end{split}

By standard short time FBSDE estimates
(see again
[
26
]
), we obtain (still under a condition of the form
CT\leq 1/2
)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big[|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}+|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}\big]+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{t}^{0,1}-Z_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{p}\biggr]\\
&\leq C\Bigl(|x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{2}|^{2p}+\mathbb{E}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2p}\Bigr]\Bigr)\\
&\leq\max(C,2C^{2})\Bigl(|x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{2}|^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}+T\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}\Big]\Bigr).\end{split}

If
\max(C,2C^{2})T\leq 1/2
(and
T\leq 1
), then

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big[|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}+|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|^{2p}\big]+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{t}^{0,1}-Z_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{p}\biggr]\\
&\leq 2\max(C,2C^{2})\Big(|x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{2}|^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}\Big).\end{split}

Plugging the above inequality into (
5.13
) and (
5.14
), we get (for a new value of
C
)

\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u_{t}^{1}-u_{t}^{2}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{t}^{1},\mu_{t}^{2})^{2p}\Big]\leq C\Big[|x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{2}|^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}\Big].

Recalling (
5.12
) and inserting the above bound, we deduce that

\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|m_{t}^{1}-m_{t}^{2}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}^{2p}\Bigr]\leq C\Big[|x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{2}|^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu^{1},\mu^{2})^{2p}\Big].\\

In summary, we obtain (
5.5
).
∎

5.2
Linearized system

We now address the following general linearized forward-backward system, set on
[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\delta X_{t}^{0}=\Big[-p_{t}\delta X_{t}^{0}-q_{t}\delta Z_{t}^{0}+a_{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\delta Y_{t}^{0}=\Big[l_{t}\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}+o_{t}\cdot\delta Z_{t}^{0}-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{t}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{t}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t}),\delta\mu_{t}\Big)+b_{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\delta Z_{t}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},

(5.15)

\displaystyle\delta X_{0}^{0}=\triangle x_{0},\quad\delta Y_{T}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T})\cdot\delta X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T}),\delta\mu_{T}\Big)+c_{T};

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\mu_{t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta\mu_{t}-{\rm div}_{x}\big(\Upsilon_{t}(x)\delta\mu_{t}+\mu_{t}\Gamma_{t}(x)\nabla_{x}\delta u_{t}(x)\big)-{\rm div}_{x}\big(d_{t}\big)=0,\quad{\rm on}\ \mathbb{T}^{d},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{t}\delta u_{t}(x)=\Big[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta u_{t}(x)+\Upsilon_{t}(x)\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta u_{t}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t}),\delta\mu_{t}\Big)+j_{t}(x)\Big]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathrm{d}_{t}\delta m_{t}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},

\displaystyle\delta\mu_{0}=\triangle\mu,\quad\delta u_{T}(x)=\nabla_{x_{0}}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})\cdot\delta X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T}),\delta\mu_{T}\Big)+k_{T}(x)\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},

which should be interpreted as a
generalized version of the system satisfied by the derivative of the flow
induced by the solution to
(
5.1
)-(
5.2
).
On top of Assumption (
Â
),
the coefficients
driving
(
5.15
)
are required to satisfy the set of conditions below:

Assumption C.

For a real
{\mathbb{r}}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
and for another real
\mathfrak{K}>1
,

(i)

The initial conditions
\triangle\mu
and
\triangle x_{0}
are deterministic,
taking values in
{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}+1}
and

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
respectively;

(ii)

The random variable
k_{T}
is
\mathcal{F}_{T}^{0}
-measurable with values in

\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{s}}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
and satisfies

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}<+\infty
;

(iii)

The process
(j_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
is
\mathbb{F}^{0}
-adapted with continuous paths
from
[0,T]
to
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

,

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}<\infty
; the process
(d_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
is
\mathbb{F}^{0}
-adapted with
continuous paths from
[0,T]
to
[{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})]^{d}
, for any

\mathbb{r}^{\prime}>\mathbb{r}

and satisfies

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}<+\infty
;

(iv)

The process

is

\mathbb{F}^{0}
-adapted with
continuous
paths from
[0,T]
to
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d}\,,\,\mathbb{R}^{d})\simeq[\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})]^{d}
, for any
{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}\in(0,{\mathbb{s}}-1)
, and satisfies

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\Upsilon_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}\leq\mathfrak{K}
;

(v)

The process
(\Gamma_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
is
\mathbb{F}^{0}
-adapted with
continuous paths from
[0,T]
to
{\mathcal{C}}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d})\simeq[{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})]^{d\times d}
; it satisfies

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{1}\leq\mathfrak{K}

and,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

\forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\quad\mathfrak{K}^{-1}I_{d}\leq\Gamma_{t}(x)\leq\mathfrak{K}I_{d};

(vi)

The processes
(a_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

and
(b_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
belong to

{\mathscr{H}}^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};\mathbb{R}^{d})

and

{\mathscr{H}}^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};\mathbb{R})
respectively;
the random variable
c_{T}
is
in
L^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{T},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};{\mathbb{R}})
;
the processes
(p_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,
(q_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,
(l_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

and
(o_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
are
\mathbb{F}^{0}
-adapted,
with respective values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
{\mathbb{R}}^{d\times d}
,

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, and satisfies

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}[|p_{t}|+|q_{t}|+|l_{t}|+|o_{t}|]\leq\mathfrak{K}
.

Within the above framework
and under the additional condition that

(d_{t})_{0<t\leq T}
takes values in
{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

for
some
\mathbb{r}^{\prime}<\mathbb{r}
,
we let, for any
p\geq 1
,

(5.16)

\begin{split}M_{p}&:=|\triangle x_{0}|^{2p}+\|\triangle\mu\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl(\|d_{t}\|^{2p}_{-\mathbb{r}+2}+\|j_{t}\|^{2p}_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}\Bigr)+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|a_{t}|+|b_{t}|\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{2p}+|c_{T}|^{2p}+\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}\biggr].\end{split}

Theorem 5.2
.

)
and
