&\leq C_{p}M_{p}.\end{split}

Remark 5.3
.

1.

In the statement of
Theorem

5.2
, measurability and progressive-measurability are understood in the same
Bochner
sense as
in the third item of Remark
2.11
. In comparison, the
novelty here is that
the forward component of the FBSPDE is also impacted by issues of measurability.
The difficulty is the same as in Definition
2.10
:
the dual space
{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}+1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

of

{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{r}-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

is not separable. The remedy is the same:
we can easily prove
(see for instance
[
14
, Lemma 3.3.1]
) that, for
t\in(0,T]
,

\delta\mu_{t}
is in fact in the subspace

{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
for some
\mathbb{r}^{\prime}<\mathbb{r}
, which is separable when
equipped
with
\|\cdot\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}
.

Actually, some care is also needed in the formulation of
Assumption (
C
). In item (ii) therein, measurability is also understood in
Bochner
sense.
In item (iii), each
j_{t}
is regarded as a random variable with values in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
for any
{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}\in(0,{\mathbb{s}}-1)
. Since
j_{t}
takes almost surely values in

{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, this says that

j_{t}
is in fact
Bochner
measurable with values in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, for any

{\mathbb{s}}^{\prime}<{\mathbb{s}}-1
. The same argument applies to
(d_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
and
(\Psi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
in items (iii) and (iv) in Assumption (
C
).

2.

In
(
5.17
),
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}

can be shown to be
measurable by the same argument as above.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, we have in fact a (deterministic) bound for
\sup_{\varepsilon\leq t\leq T}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+1}
for some
\mathbb{r}^{\prime}<\mathbb{r}
, from which
we deduce that
\delta{\bm{\mu}}
has continuous trajectories from
[\varepsilon,T]
to
{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}+1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
This proves that

\sup_{t\in[\varepsilon,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}

is measurable. Letting
\varepsilon
tend to
0
, we deduce that

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}

and
then

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}

are measurable.
That said, it must be clear that there is actually no need of measurability of the supremum to
write down the result. Instead, we can just say that there exists
C>0
such that
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}(\{\forall t\in[0,T],\ \|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}\leq C\})=1
, which is licit since
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
is complete.
In particular, this is exactly how
the condition

\textrm{\rm essup}_{\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}<+\infty

in item (iii) of (
C
)
should be understood:
formally, we cannot prove that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}

is measurable, but the condition still makes sense. (Notice that, in comparison,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}

– also in item (iii) of Assumption (
C
)—
is a random variable, see Remark

2.11
.
Here, the same argument does not hold
for processes taking values in
the dual space

{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

because the norm

\|\cdot\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}
is
not lower-semicontinuous
on
{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

for
\mathbb{r}^{\prime}>\mathbb{r}
. )

3.

The fact that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}

may not be a random variable explains why we need another condition
to define
M
in
(
5.16
) (in order to guarantee that the
sup
is in fact measurable). Indeed, if
d_{t}
takes values in
[{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})]^{d}

for some
\mathbb{r}^{\prime}<\mathbb{r}
, then (for any
\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0})

\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}=\sup_{g\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):\|g\|_{\mathbb{r}-2} \leq 1}(d_{t},g).

And then, using the fact that the process
{\bm{d}}
has continuous trajectories with values in

[{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})]^{d}
for any
\mathbb{r}^{\prime\prime}>\mathbb{r}
(see item (ii) in
Assumption (
C
)), we deduce have that, for any

g\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,

(d_{t},g)=\lim_{s\rightarrow t,s\in{\mathbb{Q}}}(d_{s},g)\leq\liminf_{s\rightarrow t,s\in{\mathbb{Q}}}\|d_{s}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2},

and then

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]\cap{\mathbb{Q}}}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}.

It then remains to prove that
each
d_{t}
is a
Bochner
random variable with values in

[{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})]^{d}
, but this follows from the fact
that it is a
random variable with values in

[{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})]^{d}

for any
\mathbb{r}^{\prime\prime}>\mathbb{r}
and that it takes values in

{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}^{\prime}+2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

for some

\mathbb{r}^{\prime}<\mathbb{r}
.

Small time unique solvability
of
(
5.15
)
is proven by means of a suitable contraction argument,
which is explained in the first two steps below.
In the third step, we establish
(
5.18
). Proceeding as in the proof of Theorem

5.1
, one can assume that

{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
is generated by

{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}
and
{\bm{B}}^{0}
.

First Step.

The first of the two steps underpinning the contraction
argument relies on the following idea.
For a given input
\delta{\bm{X}}^{0}:=(\delta X^{0}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfying
\delta X_{0}^{0}=\triangle x_{0}
,
we want to solve
the FBSPDE, on
[t,T]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}-{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\Upsilon_{t}(x)\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}+\mu_{t}\Gamma_{t}(x)\nabla_{x}\delta\tilde{u}_{t}(x)\Bigr)-{\rm div}_{x}\big(d_{t}\big)=0,\quad{\rm on}\ {\mathbb{T}}^{d},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{t}\delta\tilde{u}_{t}(x)=\Bigl[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta\tilde{u}_{t}(x)+\Upsilon_{t}(x)\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta\tilde{u}_{t}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\tilde{\mu}_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0}-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}\Big)+j_{t}(x)\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t

(5.19)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{d}_{t}\delta\tilde{m}_{t}(x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{\mu}_{0}=\triangle\mu,\quad\delta\tilde{u}_{T}(x)=\nabla_{x_{0}}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})\cdot\delta X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}\Big)+k_{T}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},

with paths in the space
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T]\,,\,\mathcal{C}^{-\mathbb{u}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{u}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{u}-2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
and with

(5.20)

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\{\|\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{u}+1}^{2}+\|\delta\tilde{u}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{u}}^{2}+\|\delta\tilde{m}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{u}-2}\right\}\biggr]<+\infty.

Basically,
we would like to
apply
[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]
in order to guarantee that there is a unique solution

(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\tilde{\bm{u}}},\delta{\tilde{\bm{m}}})
, adapted with respect to the filtration
\mathbb{F}^{0}
, to
(
5.19
)–(
5.20
).
Notice that if we can indeed apply
[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]
,
then

Bochner
measurability
with values in any
\mathcal{C}^{-\mathbb{u}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{u}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{u}-2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is obvious because

[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]
then implies that the solution is also
in
\mathcal{C}^{-\mathbb{u}^{\prime\prime}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{u}^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{u}^{\prime}-2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
for
\mathbb{r}<\mathbb{u}^{\prime\prime}<\mathbb{u}<\mathbb{u}^{\prime}<\mathbb{s}
.

Actually, the difficulty is that
the current setting
does not exactly fit the assumption
of

[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]
.
One first difficulty is that

[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]

is stated in the monotone framework.
This is the same issue as the one mentioned in the proof of
Theorem

5.1

and the remedy is very similar. Here is an overview of it. Because of the lack of monotonicity,
there is an additional term in

[
14
, (4.44)]

that writes
(with the notations
from
[
14
]
)

{\mathbb{E}}[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\tilde{\rho}_{t}-\tilde{\rho}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{-(n+\alpha^{\prime})}^{2}]

(in our system of notations,
\tilde{\rho}_{t}

corresponds to

\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}
,
\tilde{\rho}_{t}^{\prime}

to another (forward) solution

\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{\prime}
and

n+\alpha^{\prime}
to
{\mathbb{u}}-1
, for

{\mathbb{u}}\in(\mathbb{r},\mathbb{s})
).
If
T
is small enough, then
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields an additional factor

\sqrt{T}
in

[
14
, (4.47)]

so that

[
14
, (4.49)]

remains true, with an additional factor

T
in front of

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\tilde{z}_{t}-\tilde{z}_{t}^{\prime}\|_{n+1+\alpha}^{2}

(again with the notations
from

[
14
]
,

\tilde{z}_{t}
being here understood as

\delta\tilde{u}_{t}
). This makes it possible to apply the same contraction
argument as in the proof of

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.7]
, the role
of
\varepsilon
(which is assumed to be small therein) being now played by
\sqrt{T}
.

Another difference is that the
process
\delta{\bm{X}}^{0}
(which appears here both in the driver and in the boundary condition of the backward equation) is not bounded (in
\omega^{0}
).
This creates another difficulty since the perturbations

\tilde{f}_{t}^{0}
and
\tilde{g}_{T}^{0}
in

[
14
, (4.37)]
are assumed to be bounded.
In order to construct a solution, we thus apply

[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]

to an
approximation of
(
5.19
), in which

(\delta X_{t}^{0})_{0\leq t\leq T}
is replaced by

(\varphi_{R}(\delta X^{0}_{t}))_{0\leq t\leq T}
for a
real
R>0
and a function

\varphi_{R}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
that is bounded by
2R
and equal to the identity mapping (from

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
into itself) on the
d
-dimensional ball of center
0
and radius
R
.
This makes it possible to combine

[
14
, Theorem 4.4.2]

and

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]
:
The first statement shows that there is a solution for each
R
(and for a fixed initial condition),
denoted

(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{R},\delta\tilde{\bm{u}}^{R},\delta\tilde{\bm{m}}^{R})
,
and the second one shows (by comparing the solutions for two different values of
R
)
that the family of (hence constructed) solutions
is Cauchy as
R
tends to
\infty
. Here, the Cauchy property is understood in the same

L^{2}
sense
as in
(
5.20
). The limit of the Cauchy sequence is a solution
of
(
5.19
). Uniqueness is slightly more challenging. We take one solution
to
(
5.19
)
satisfying
(
5.20
). We then introduce the stopping
time

\tau_{R}:=\inf\{t\in[0,T]:|\delta X_{t}^{0}|+\|\delta\tilde{u}_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}\geq R\}
.
Writing the system
solved by

(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}},\delta\tilde{\bm{u}}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}},\delta\tilde{\bm{m}}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}})_{0\leq t\leq T}

as a forward-backward system on
[0,T]
,
with

\delta\tilde{u}_{\tau_{R}}(x)=\Bigl[\nabla_{x_{0}}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})\cdot\delta X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}\Big)+k_{T}(x)\Bigr]{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\tau_{R}=T\}}+\delta\tilde{u}_{\tau_{R}}(x){\mathbf{1}}_{\{\tau_{R}<T\}},\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},

as terminal condition,
we can compare

(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}},\delta\tilde{\bm{u}}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}},\delta\tilde{\bm{v}}^{0}_{t\wedge\tau_{R}})_{0\leq t\leq T}

with

(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{R},\delta\tilde{\bm{u}}^{R},\delta\tilde{\bm{v}}^{0,R})
by means of

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]
. Thanks to the above decomposition, we observe that, on the event

\{\tau_{R}=T\}
,
the terminal condition of the forward-backward system obtained by making the difference of the two solutions
just writes

(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}-\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}^{R})

and
is thus purely linear
(equivalently, the intercept is zero).
On the event
\{\tau_{R}<T\}
,
the terminal condition writes
[\delta\tilde{u}_{\tau_{R}}(x)-\delta\tilde{u}_{T}^{R}(x)]{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\tau_{R}<T\}}
, which tends, by
a standard uniform integrability argument, to
0
in
L^{2}
as
R
tends to
0
.
By

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]
,
this proves uniqueness. Also, this makes it possible to compare any two solutions satisfying
(
5.19
)
and (
5.20
)
by means of

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]
again.
All these claims hold true for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
where

{\mathfrak{C}}>0
only depends
on

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{K}}
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\sigma_{0}
and
(\mathbb{r},{\mathbb{s}})
. In particular,

{\mathfrak{C}}
is independent of the initial condition.

Next we define
(\delta\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta\tilde{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{0})
as the strong solution to the following FBSDE system (on
[0,T]
)

(5.21)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}=\Big[-p_{t}\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}-q_{t}\delta\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0}+a_{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t,\\
&\mathrm{d}\delta\tilde{Y}_{t}^{0}=\Big[l_{t}\cdot\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}+o_{t}\delta\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0}\\
&\hskip 45.0pt-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{t}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\cdot\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{t}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}\Big)+b_{t}\Big]\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\delta\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T],\\
&\delta\tilde{X}_{0}^{0}=\triangle x_{0},\quad\delta\tilde{Y}_{T}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T})\cdot\delta\tilde{X}_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}\Big)+c_{T}.\end{split}

Here, solutions are required to satisfy

(5.22)

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigl(|\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}|^{2}+|\delta\tilde{Y}_{t}^{0}|^{2}\bigr)+\int_{0}^{T}|\delta\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]<\infty.

By
[
26
]
, existence and uniqueness of a solution hold true for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
, for
a possibly new value of the constant
{\mathfrak{C}}
.
Next, we implicitly require
T
to be less than
{\mathfrak{C}}
.

Setting
\delta{\mathfrak{T}}(\delta X^{0}):=\delta\tilde{X}^{0}
, this defines a map
\delta{\mathfrak{T}}:{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Second Step.

In this step, we show that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
(again for a possibly new value of

{\mathfrak{C}}
, depending on
d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{K}}
,

{\mathfrak{L}}
and
({\mathbb{r}},{\mathbb{s}})
),
\delta{\mathfrak{T}}
is a contraction mapping from
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

For
two inputs
\delta{\bm{X}}^{0,1}
and
\delta{\bm{X}}^{0,2}
with
\delta X^{0,1}_{0}=\delta X^{0,2}_{0}=\triangle x_{0}

as initial conditions, we denote
by
(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{1},\delta\tilde{\bm{u}}^{1},\delta\tilde{\bm{m}}^{1})
and
(\delta\tilde{\bm{\mu}}^{2},\delta\tilde{\bm{u}}^{2},\delta\tilde{\bm{m}}^{2})

the corresponding solutions to
(
5.19
), and next by
(\delta\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0,1},\delta\tilde{\bm{Y}}^{0,1},\delta\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{0,1})
and
(\delta\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0,2},\delta\tilde{\bm{Y}}^{0,2},\delta\tilde{\bm{Z}}^{0,2})
the corresponding solutions to (
5.21
).
Using

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]

(which is licit thanks to the analysis performed in
the first step,
and
with the following notations therein:
the primed solution
is null,

\vartheta=1
,

\tilde{m}_{t}=\mu_{t}
,

\tilde{z}_{t}(x)=\delta\tilde{u}_{t}^{1}(x)
,

\tilde{z}_{t}^{\prime}(x)=\delta\tilde{u}_{t}^{2}(x)
,

\tilde{f}_{t}^{0}(x)=j_{t}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\tilde{\mu}_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0,1}
,

\tilde{f}_{t}^{0,\prime}(x)=j_{t}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{t}(X_{t}^{0},x,\tilde{\mu}_{t})\cdot\delta X_{t}^{0,2}
,

\tilde{V}_{t}(x)=\tilde{V}_{t}^{\prime}(x)=\Upsilon_{t}(x)
,

\tilde{b}_{t}^{0}(x)=\tilde{b}_{t}^{0,\prime}(x)=d_{t}(x)
), we get
(thanks to
Remark
5.3
,
the term inside the expectation symbol in the left-hand side below is
a random variable):

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\tilde{\mu}^{1}_{t}-\delta\tilde{\mu}^{2}_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2}\bigg]\leq C\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta X_{t}^{0,1}-\delta X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\bigg],\end{split}

where
C
only depends on

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{K}}
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
, and
(\mathbb{r},{\mathbb{s}})
.
By standard short time stability estimates for FBSDEs, see

[
26
, Theorem 1.3]
,
we can derive (for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
, allowing the value of

{\mathfrak{C}}
to vary from line to line as long as it just depends on the same parameters as before)

(5.23)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}|\delta\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0,1}-\delta\tilde{Z}_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\\
&\leq C\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\delta_{\mu}f^{0}_{t}(X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{1}-\delta\tilde{\mu}_{t}^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}t\bigg)^{2}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T}),\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}^{1}-\delta\tilde{\mu}_{T}^{2}\Big)^{2}\biggr]\\
&\leq C\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\tilde{\mu}^{1}_{t}-\delta\tilde{\mu}^{2}_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2}\bigg]\leq C\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\bigg].\end{split}

Inserting the above estimate in the forward equation of
(
5.21
), we deduce that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,

\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0,1}-\delta\tilde{X}_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\biggr]\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta X_{t}^{0,1}-\delta X_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}\biggr],

which implies
\delta{\mathfrak{T}}
is a contraction mapping from
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Therefore, there exists a unique fixed point

\delta{\bm{X}}^{0}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\delta X_{0}^{0}=\triangle x_{0}
.

Then, the coupled system comprising
both
the (finite-dimensional) FBSDE and
the
FBSPDE in (
5.15
) admits a unique solution, which is adapted with respect to the filtration
\mathbb{F}^{0}
. The solution to the FBSPDE system is denoted

(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{v}}^{0})
. It satisfies

(a)
and
(b)
in the statement. The
solution to the finite-dimensional FBSDE system in (
5.15
) admits a unique strong solution
(\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})
.
It satisfies
(c)
in the statement.

Third Step.
We now estimate the 6-tuple
(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})
, solution to the forward-backward system (
5.15
).
The proof is similar to that one of

[
14
, Corollary 4.4.6]
.
The point is to compare the solution

(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}})
of the FBSPDE system in
(
5.15
)
with the trivial system driven by null coefficients and thus having
a null solution. Comparison is obtained by means of

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]
.
One of the key point in the proof is
to fix one instant
t\in[0,T]

and then
to apply

[
14
, Proposition 4.4.5]

to the restriction of the
triple

(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}})

to the interval
[t,T]
, seen
as a solution
of the FBSPDE system in
(
5.15
)
on
[t,T]
, with
\delta\mu_{t}
as initial solution
and
under the conditional
probability distribution of

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
given the
\sigma
-field

{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}
.

We get, with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,

\begin{split}\|\delta u_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2}\leq C\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2}+C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\|d_{r}\|^{2}_{-\mathbb{r}+2}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\|j_{r}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}|\delta X_{r}^{0}|^{2}+\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\Bigr],\end{split}

where
C

only depends on

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{K}}
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
and
(\mathbb{r},{\mathbb{s}})
(recalling that
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
) and is allowed to vary from line to line.
Measurability of the arguments in the above right-hand side
follows from
the third item in Remark

5.3
.

Taking
power
p\in(1,8]

and then expectation (under
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
) on both sides, we obtain
by means of Doob’s inequality,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta u_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}\Big]\\
&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|^{2p}_{-\mathbb{r}+2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j_{t}\|^{2p}_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta X_{t}^{0}|^{2p}+\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}\Bigr].\end{split}

Now, by an obvious adaptation of Lemma

6.3
(see for instance

[
14
, Lemma 3.3.1]
),
we have,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}\leq C\|\triangle\mu\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}+CT\Bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta u_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+2}\Bigr].

And, then,
assuming
CT\leq 1/2
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}\Big]\leq C\Bigl(\|\triangle\mu\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|d_{t}\|^{2p}_{-\mathbb{r}+2}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|j_{t}\|^{2p}_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\delta X_{t}^{0}|^{2p}+\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}\Bigr]\Bigr).\end{split}

And then, by
[
26
, Theorem A.5]
,

\begin{split} &{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigg[\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}|\delta Z_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{p}\biggr]\\
&\leq C\biggl(|\triangle x_{0}|^{2p}+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\delta\mu_{t}\|^{2p}_{-\mathbb{r}+1}+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|a_{t}|+|b_{t}|\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{2p}+|c_{T}|^{2p}\biggr]\biggr)\\
&\leq C\biggl(|\triangle x_{0}|^{2p}+\|\triangle\mu\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl(\|d_{t}\|^{2p}_{-\mathbb{r}+2}+\|j_{t}\|^{2p}_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}+|\delta X_{t}^{0}|^{2p}\Bigr)+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|a_{t}|+|b_{t}|\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{2p}+|c_{T}|^{2p}+\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}\biggr]\biggr).\end{split}

Inserting the above bound into the equation for

\delta{\bm{X}}^{0}
, we deduce that, for
CT\leq 1/2
(and
T\leq 1
),

\begin{split} &{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\delta X_{t}^{0}|^{2p}\Bigr]\\
&\leq C\biggl(|\triangle x_{0}|^{2p}+\|\triangle\mu\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Bigl(\|d_{t}\|^{2p}_{-\mathbb{r}+2}+\|j_{t}\|^{2p}_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}\Bigr)+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|a_{t}|+|b_{t}|\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{2p}+|c_{T}|^{2p}+\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}\biggr]\biggr)\\
&=CM_{p},\end{split}

and the proof is easily completed by recombining all the
intermediary steps.
∎

5.3
First order derivatives of the master fields

For simplicity, the results in the last two subsections are stated with
t=0
as initial time. However, they remain true for any initial time
t\in[0,T]
(provided

T
is small enough). In particular, for any initial condition
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, the system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) has a unique solution, denoted
(X^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},Y^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},Z^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},\mu^{t,x_{0},\mu},u^{t,x_{0},\mu},m^{t,x_{0},\mu})
, on
[t,T]
with
T
given in Theorem
5.1
, which makes it possible to let

(5.24)

{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu):=u^{t,x_{0},\mu}(t,x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},

and

(5.25)

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu):=Y^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}_{t}.

Elaborating on
the proof of
[
14
, Lemma 5.1.1]

and using
the uniqueness result of (
5.1
)–(
5.2
)
together with the stability property (
5.5
), we have, with probability 1 under

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,

(5.26)

u^{t,x_{0},\mu}(r,x)={\mathcal{U}}(r,X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},x,\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu})\quad\text{and}\quad Y_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}={\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu}),\quad\text{for any }r\in[t,T].

Thanks to
(
5.3
)
and
(
5.4
),
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
are bounded.
In fact, by (
5.5
) again, we also have

(5.27)

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\ni(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\ \text{ is uniformly Lipschitz continuous in $x_{0},\mu$},\\
&{\mathcal{U}}^{0}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\ni(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}\ \text{ is uniformly Lipschitz continuous in $x_{0},\mu$},\\
\end{split}

where the Lipschitz continuity in
\mu
is measured by the
\mathbb{W}_{1}
metric.
Continuity in time is ensured by the following lemma:

Lemma 5.4
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
, there exists a constant

{\mathfrak{C}}
only depending on

d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
and
{\mathbb{s}}
,
such that the following holds true for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
.
For any
{\mathbb{r}}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
, we can find a constant
C
, only depending on

d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,
\sigma_{0}
,
{\mathbb{r}}

and

{\mathbb{s}}
, such that, for any
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
r\in[t,T]
,

(5.28)

\|{\mathcal{U}}(r,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)-{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}\leq C(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/2},

and

(5.29)

|{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,x_{0},\mu)-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)|\leq C(r-t)^{1/2}.

First Step.
We first show (
5.28
).
Following
[
14
, (4.12)]
, note that, with probability 1 under
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,

u_{t}(\cdot)=\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[P_{r-t}u_{r}(\cdot)-\int_{t}^{r}P_{s-t}\Bigl(\hat{H}(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{s}(\cdot))-f_{s}(X_{s}^{0},\cdot,\mu_{s})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\bigg],\quad\text{for any }r\in[t,T],

where
(P_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
stands here for the standard heat kernel on the torus
\mathbb{T}^{d}
.
The expectation in the right-hand side should be understood as follows:
here and below, we write
{\mathbb{E}}^{0}[h(\cdot)]
for the mapping

x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}^{0}[h(x)]
when

h:\Omega^{0}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

is a (measurable) random field such that
{\mathbb{E}}^{0}[|h(x)|]
is finite for any
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
.
Of course, Lebesgue’s theorem makes it possible to address the regularity of

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}[h(\cdot)]

when
h
itself is regular in
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
.
Also, we recall
from
(
5.4
)
that there exists a constant
C
, only depending
on the parameters in (
Â
), such that

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}(\{\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}\|\nabla_{x}u_{s}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}\leq C\})=1
.
And then (with
{\rm id}
denoting the identity mapping),

u_{t}(\cdot)-\mathbb{E}^{0}[u_{r}(\cdot)]=\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\Big(P_{r-t}-{\rm id}\Big)u_{r}(\cdot)-\int_{t}^{r}P_{s-t}\Bigl(\hat{H}(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{s}(\cdot))-f_{s}(X_{s}^{0},\cdot,\mu_{s})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\bigg],

which further implies

\begin{split}\big\|u_{t}-\mathbb{E}^{0}[u_{r}]\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}&\leq\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\big\|(P_{r-t}-{\rm id})u_{r}\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}+C\int_{t}^{r}(s-t)^{-1/2}\big\|\hat{H}(\cdot,\nabla_{x}u_{s}(\cdot))-f_{s}(X_{s}^{0},\cdot,\mu_{s})\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\big\|(P_{r-t}-{\rm id})u_{r}\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}\bigg]+C(r-t)^{1/2},\end{split}

with the constant
C
only depends on the parameters in Assumption (
Â
).
It is standard to have

\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\big\|(P_{r-t}-{\rm id})u_{r}\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}\Big]\leq C(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/{2}}\mathbb{E}^{0}\big[\|u_{r}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}\big]\leq C(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/{2}}.

Therefore,
allowing the constant
C
to vary from line to line,

\big\|u_{t}-\mathbb{E}^{0}[u_{r}]\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}\leq C(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/{2}}.

By (
5.24
), we can derive

\begin{split}\big\|u_{t}-{\mathcal{U}}(r,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}&\leq\big\|u_{t}-\mathbb{E}^{0}[u_{r}]\big\|_{r}+\Big\|\mathbb{E}^{0}[u_{r}]-{\mathcal{U}}(r,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\Big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}\\
&\leq C(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/{2}}+\Big\|\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[{\mathcal{U}}(r,X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},\cdot,\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}(r,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\Big]\Big\|_{{\mathbb{r}}}\\
&\leq C\Big\{(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/{2}}+\mathbb{E}^{0}\big[\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu},\mu)+|X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}-x_{0}|\big]\Big\}\\
&\leq C(r-t)^{({\mathbb{s}}-{\mathbb{r}})/{2}}.\end{split}

Second Step.
We now show (
5.29
). We first have

(5.30)

\begin{split}&\big|{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,x_{0},\mu)-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\big|\\
&\leq\bigl|{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,x_{0},\mu)-\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu})\bigr]\bigr|+\bigl|\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu},\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\bigr]\bigr|\\
&\leq C\Bigl(\mathbb{E}^{0}\big[\mathbb{W}_{1}(\mu_{r}^{t,x_{0},\mu},\mu)+|X_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}-x_{0}|\big]+\big|\mathbb{E}^{0}\big[Y_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}-Y_{t}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}\big]\big|\Bigr)\\
&\leq C\Bigl(|r-t|^{1/2}+\big|\mathbb{E}^{0}\big[Y_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}-Y_{t}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}\big]\big|\Bigr).\end{split}

By means of
(
5.3
), we
have a bound for
{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{t}^{r}|Z_{s}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s
, from which we obtain

(5.31)

\mathbb{E}^{0}\big[\big|Y_{r}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}-Y_{t}^{0,t,x_{0},\mu}\big|\big]\leq C|r-t|^{1/2}.

Combining (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), we can derive

|{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(r,x_{0},\mu)-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)|\leq C(r-t)^{1/2},

which completes the proof.
∎

We now would like to establish the continuous differentiability of
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}
in
x_{0}
and
\mu
. For any initial condition
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
(\triangle x_{0},\triangle\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{C}^{-\mathbb{r}+1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
for some
\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
,
we consider the solution
(\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0},\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}})
(its dependence on the initial condition is omitted for simplicity) to the linearized forward-backward
system, set on
[t,T]
,

(5.32)

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}\delta X_{r}^{0}=\Big[-\nabla^{2}_{px_{0}}H^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\delta X_{r}^{0}-\nabla^{2}_{pp}H^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\delta Z_{r}^{0}\Big]\mathrm{d}r,\\
&\mathrm{d}\delta Y_{r}^{0}=\Big[\nabla_{x_{0}}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\cdot\delta X_{r}^{0}+\nabla_{p}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\cdot\delta Z_{r}^{0}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r})\cdot\delta X_{r}^{0}-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r}),\delta\mu_{r}\Big)\Big]\mathrm{d}r+\sigma_{0}\delta Z_{r}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{r}^{0},\\
&\delta X_{t}^{0}=\triangle x_{0},\quad\delta Y_{T}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T})\cdot\delta X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T}),\delta\mu_{T}\Big);\\
&\partial_{r}\delta\mu_{r}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta\mu_{r}-{\rm div}_{x}\big(\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\delta\mu_{r}+\mu_{r}\nabla_{pp}^{2}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\nabla_{x}\delta u_{r}(x)\big)=0,\\
&\mathrm{d}_{r}\delta u_{r}(x)=\Big[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta u_{r}(x)+\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta u_{r}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r})\cdot\delta X_{r}^{0}\\
&\hskip 45.0pt-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r}),\delta\mu_{r}\Big)\Big]\mathrm{d}r+\mathrm{d}_{r}\delta m_{r}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\\
&\delta\mu_{t}=\triangle\mu,\quad\delta u_{T}(x)=\nabla_{x_{0}}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})\cdot\delta X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T}),\delta\mu_{T}\Big)\quad\text{on }\mathbb{T}^{d},\end{split}

where
({\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0},{\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{m}})
is the unique solution to the system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) corresponding to the initial condition
(t,x_{0},\mu)
. Applying Theorem
5.2
to (
5.32
), we have the following theorem.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
{\mathbb{r}}\in(\lfloor\mathbb{s}\rfloor,\mathbb{s})
.
Under Assumption
(
Â
)
,
there exist a constant

c
only depending on
d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
and

\mathbb{s}

and a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}
only depending on
d
,
\kappa
,

{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta(c)
,
\sigma_{0}
and

({\mathbb{r}},\mathbb{s})

such that,
for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
the forward-backward system (
5.15
) admits a unique solution
(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})
, adapted with respect to the filtration
\mathbb{F}^{0}
and with values in

{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}+1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{s}}-2}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
and satisfying
items (a), (b) and (c) in the statement of Theorem

5.2
.

Moreover,
there exists a constant
C
only depending on the parameters in
Assumption
(
Â
)
and on
{\mathbb{r}}
such that,
for any
p\in[1,8]
,

(5.33)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl(\|\delta\mu_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}+\|\delta u_{t}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}+\|\delta m_{t}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}^{2p}\Bigr)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigl(|\delta X_{t}^{0}|^{2p}+|\delta Y_{t}^{0}|^{2p}\Bigr)+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}|\delta Z_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{p}\biggr]\\
&\leq C\big(\|\delta\mu\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}+|\triangle x_{0}|^{2p}\big).\end{split}

The only subtlety in the application of
Theorem
5.2

lies in the verification of (v) in
Assumption (
C
).
In fact, by Theorem

5.1
,
we have (for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
) a bound for

\|u_{t}\|_{\mathbb{s}}
. And then, we can insert this bound in the
function
\zeta
in Assumption (
Â4
).
The rest of the proof does not raise any difficulty.
∎

Given
(t,x_{0},y,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
i\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
and a
d
-tuple
l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1\}^{d}
with
|l|:=\sum_{j=1}^{d}l_{j}\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
, we first denote by
(\delta{\bm{\mu}}^{i},\delta{\bm{u}}^{i},\delta{\bm{m}}^{i},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0,i},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0,i},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0,i})
the solution to the system (
5.32
) on
[t,T]
with
\triangle\mu=0
and
\triangle x^{0}=e_{i}
, and then denote by

(\delta{\bm{\mu}}^{l,y},\delta{\bm{u}}^{l,y},\delta{\bm{m}}^{l,y},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0,l,y},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0,l,y},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0,l,y})
the solution to the system (
5.32
) on
[t,T]
with
\triangle\mu=(-1)^{|l|}\nabla^{l}\delta_{y}\in\mathcal{C}^{-(\mathbb{r}-1)}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
(
\delta_{y}
denoting the Dirac mass at point
y
) for any

\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
, and
\triangle x^{0}=0
.
Define

(5.34)

\begin{split}&K_{i}^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu):=\delta u_{t}^{i}(x),\,\,K_{i}^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu):=\delta Y_{t}^{0,i},\\
&K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y):=\delta u_{t}^{l,y}(x),\,\,K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y):=\delta Y_{t}^{0,l,y}.\end{split}

Then, choosing for instance

{\mathbb{r}}=(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor+{\mathbb{s}})/2
,
we can apply Theorem
5.5
to obtain
(for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
, for some
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
only depending
on the parameters

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption (
Â
))

(5.35)

\|K_{i}^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\|_{\mathbb{s}}+\|K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\|_{\mathbb{s}}+|K_{i}^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)|+|K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)|\leq C,

for a constant
C
only depending on the parameters in
Assumption (
Â
).
In what follows, we just write
K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)
and

K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)

for the
d
-tuples

(K_{i}^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu))_{i=1,\cdots,d}
and

(K_{i}^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu))_{i=1,\cdots,d}
.

The following statement is the analogue of
[
14
, Lemma 5.2.2]
:

Lemma 5.6
.

Given
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
p\in[1,8]
, we have under the same generic condition
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
as before, for any
d
-tuple
l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1\}^{d}
with
|l|:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}l_{i}\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
and any
y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,

(5.36)

\begin{split}\lim_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{d},h\to 0}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\big\|\delta u_{r}^{l,y+h}-\delta u_{r}^{l,y}\big\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\big\|\delta\mu_{r}^{l,y+h}-\delta\mu_{r}^{l,y}\big\|_{-{\mathbb{s}}+1}^{2p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\big|\delta Y_{r}^{0,l,y+h}-\delta Y_{r}^{0,l,y}\big|^{2p}\Big]=0.\end{split}

Moreover, for any
l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-2\}^{d}
with
|l|\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-2
, and any
i\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
,

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\in\mathbb{R},h\to 0}\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Bigl\|\frac{1}{h}(\delta u_{r}^{l,y+he_{i}}-\delta u_{r}^{l,y})-\delta u_{r}^{l+e_{i},y}\Big\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Big\|\frac{1}{h}(\delta\mu_{r}^{l,y+he_{i}}-\delta\mu_{r}^{l,y})-\delta\mu_{r}^{l+e_{i},y}\Big\|_{-{\mathbb{s}}+1}^{2p}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Big|\frac{1}{h}(\delta Y_{r}^{0,l,y+he_{i}}-\delta Y_{r}^{0,l,y})-\delta Y_{r}^{0,l+e_{i},y}\Big|^{2p}\Big]=0.\end{split}

In particular, the function
(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto K_{0}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)
and
y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)
are
(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1)
-times continuously differentiable with respect to
y
, and for any
l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1\}
with
|l|\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
, the derivative
y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\mapsto\nabla_{y}^{l}K_{0}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{s}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
is continuous. It holds, for any

(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
,

\nabla_{y}^{l}K^{\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)=K^{\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla_{y}^{l}K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)=K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y).

Moreover, there exists a constant
C
, only depending on the parameters in Assumption
(
Â
)
, such that

\sup_{(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}}\big\|K^{\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\big\|_{\mathbb{s}}\leq C,

and

\sup_{(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}}\big|K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\big|\leq C.

By Theorem
5.5
,
for
any fixed
\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})

and
any
p\in[1,8]
,
we can find a constant
C_{p}
such that, for all
(t,x,\mu,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
and
l\in\{1,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1\}
with
|l|\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
,

\mathbb{E}^{0}\Big[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\big\|\delta u_{r}^{l,y}\big\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2p}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\big\|\delta\mu_{r}^{l,y}\big\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\big|\delta Y_{r}^{0,l,y}\big|^{2p}\Big]\leq C_{p}.

In particular,

\|K^{\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\|_{\mathbb{s}}+|K^{0,\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)|\leq C.

Notice that

\lim_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{d},h\to 0}\big\|\nabla^{l}_{y}\delta_{y+h}-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{y}\big\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}=0.

Therefore, Theorem
5.5
gives (
5.36
). This yields

\lim_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{d},h\to 0}\Big(\big\|K^{\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y+h)-K^{\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\big\|_{\mathbb{s}}+\big|K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y+h)-K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\big|\Big)=0,

which implies
that the mappings
y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto K^{\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{s}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

and

y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto K^{0,\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\in\mathbb{R}
are continuous.
Similarly, for any
l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-2\}^{d}
with
|l|\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-2
, and any
i\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
,

\lim_{h\in\mathbb{R},h\to 0}\Big\|\frac{1}{h}\big(\nabla^{l}\delta_{y+he_{i}}-\nabla^{l}\delta_{y}\big)+\nabla^{l+e_{i}}\delta_{y}\Big\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}=0.

Therefore, again by Theorem
5.5
, we get

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\in\mathbb{R},h\to 0}\bigg(\Big\|\frac{1}{h}\big(K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y+he_{i})-K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\big)-K^{\mu}_{l+e_{i}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\Big\|_{\mathbb{s}}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\Big|\frac{1}{h}\big(K^{0,\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\mu,y+he_{i})-K^{0,\mu}_{l}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\big)-K^{0,\mu}_{l+e_{i}}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\Big|\bigg)=0,\end{split}

which implies, by induction, that

\nabla_{y}^{l}K^{\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)=K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla_{y}^{l}K^{0,\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)=K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y).

The proof is completed.
∎

The following statement is the analogue of

[
14
, Lemma 5.2.3]
:

Lemma 5.7
.

Given
\triangle x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a finite signed measure
\triangle\mu
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the solution
(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})
to the system (
5.32
) has the following representation formulas

(5.37)

\delta u_{t}(x)=K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}K_{0}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y),

and

(5.38)

\delta Y_{t}^{0}=K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y).

By compactness of the torus, we can find, for a given
\varepsilon>0
, a covering
\{U_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
of
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, made of disjoint Borel subsets with diameter less than
\varepsilon
. Choosing, for each
i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
,
y_{i}\in U_{i}
, we then let

\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\triangle\mu(U_{i})\delta_{y_{i}}.

Then,

\begin{split}\|\triangle\mu-\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}\|_{-1}&=\sup_{\|\phi\|_{1}\leq 1}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\phi(y)\big(\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y)-\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}y)\big)\bigg|\\
&=\sup_{\|\phi\|_{1}\leq 1}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}\big(\phi(y)-\phi(y_{i})\big)\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg|\leq C|\triangle\mu|_{\rm TV}\varepsilon,\end{split}

where
|\triangle\mu|_{\rm TV}
is the total mass of
\triangle\mu
.
Consider now
(
5.15
)
with

(\triangle x_{0},\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon})
as initial condition at time

t

and denote the corresponding solution by

(\delta{\bm{\mu}}^{\varepsilon},\delta{\bm{u}}^{\varepsilon},\delta{\bm{m}}^{\varepsilon},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0,\varepsilon},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0,\varepsilon},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0,\varepsilon})
.
By linearity of the system
(
5.32
), we observe that

\begin{split}\delta Y_{t}^{0,\varepsilon}&=K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}K^{0,\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y_{i})\triangle\mu(U_{i})\\
&=K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}K^{0,\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y_{i})\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y),\\
\delta u_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)&=K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}K^{\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y_{i})\triangle\mu(U_{i})\\
&=K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}K^{\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y_{i})\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}.\end{split}

And
then, by invoking Theorem
5.5
in order to compare

(\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0})

and

(\delta{\bm{u}}^{\varepsilon},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0,\varepsilon})
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\bigg\|\delta u_{t}(\cdot)-K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}K^{\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y_{i})\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}\\
&+\bigg|\delta Y_{t}^{0}-K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y_{i})\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg|\\
&\leq C\|\triangle\mu-\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}\|_{-{\mathbb{s}}+1}\leq C\|\triangle\mu-\triangle\mu^{\varepsilon}\|_{-1}\leq C|\triangle\mu|_{\rm TV}\varepsilon.\end{split}

By the smoothness of
K^{\mu}_{0}
and
K_{0}^{0,\mu}
derived in Lemma
5.6
, we can easily derive that

(5.39)

\begin{split}&\bigg\|\delta u_{t}(\cdot)-K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}K^{\mu}_{0}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}\\
&+\bigg|\delta Y_{t}^{0}-K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot\triangle x_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{U_{i}}K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\triangle\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg|\\
&\leq C|\triangle\mu|_{\rm TV}\varepsilon.\end{split}

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
in (
5.39
), we obtain (
5.37
) and (
5.38
).
∎

5.4
Differentiability of the fields
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
in the variables
x_{0}
and

\mu

The following statement provides a second-order expansion of the
fields
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and

{\mathcal{U}}
:

Proposition 5.8
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
, consider two initial conditions
(t,x_{0},\mu),(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
call

({\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{m}},{\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0})

and
(\hat{\bm{\mu}},\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{m}},\hat{\bm{X}}^{0},\hat{\bm{Y}}^{0},\hat{\bm{Z}}^{0})
the solutions of the system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) with
(t,x_{0},\mu)
and
(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})
as
respective initial conditions and
(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})
the solution of the system (
5.32
) with
(t,\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0},\hat{\mu}-\mu)
as
initial condition, so that we can let

\begin{split}&\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{X}}^{0}:=\hat{\bm{X}}^{0}-{\bm{X}}^{0}-\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\quad\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{Y}}^{0}:=\hat{\bm{Y}}^{0}-{\bm{Y}}^{0}-\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\quad\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{Z}}^{0}:=\hat{\bm{Z}}^{0}-{\bm{Z}}^{0}-\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0},\\
&\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{\mu}}:=\hat{\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}-\delta{\bm{\mu}},\quad\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{u}}:=\hat{\bm{u}}-{\bm{u}}-\delta{\bm{u}},\quad\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{m}}:=\hat{\bm{m}}-{\bm{m}}-\delta{\bm{m}}.\end{split}

Let
\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
.
Then,
for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
for some
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
only depending on the parameters

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

{\mathbb{s}}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
and on
{\mathbb{r}}
,
we can find a constant
C
, only depending
on the parameters in Assumption
(
Â
)
and on
{\mathbb{r}}
,
such that, for any

p\in[1,8]
,

(5.40)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Bigl(\|\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}\mu_{r}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{p}+\|\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}u_{r}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{p}+\|\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}m_{r}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}^{p}\Bigr)\bigg]\\
&+\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}|\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{r}^{0}|^{p}+\sup_{r\in[t,T]}|\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Y_{t}^{0}|^{p}+\int_{t}^{T}|\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Z_{r}^{0}|^{p}\mathrm{d}r\bigg]\leq C_{p}\Big(\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu},\mu)^{2p}+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2p}\Big).\end{split}

In particular,
by choosing
p=1
,
we get (with
C:=C_{1}
)

\begin{split}&\bigg\|{\mathcal{U}}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\cdot,\hat{\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)-K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}K_{0}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y))\bigg\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}\\
&\leq C\Big(\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu},\mu)^{2}+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2}\Big),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\bigg|{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)-K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)(\hat{\mu}-\mu)(\mathrm{d}y)\bigg|\\
&\leq C\Big(\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu},\mu)^{2}+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2}\Big),\end{split}

and, thus, for any
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the mappings

(x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\quad{\rm and}\quad(x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)

are differentiable with respect to
x_{0}
and
\mu
and the derivatives read, for any
(x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,

\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)=K^{x_{0}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\quad\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)=K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu),

and

\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)=K_{0}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y),\quad\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)=K_{0}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y),\quad\text{for any }y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}.

We first note that
(\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{X}}^{0},\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{Y}}^{0},\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{Z}}^{0})
solves, on

[t,T]
,

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}_{r}\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{r}^{0}=\big(-\nabla_{px_{0}}H^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{r}^{0}-\nabla_{pp}H^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Z_{r}^{0}+a_{r}\big)\mathrm{d}r,\\
&\mathrm{d}_{r}\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Y_{r}^{0}=\bigg[-\nabla_{x_{0}}f^{0}_{r}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r})\cdot\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{r}^{0}-\big(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r},\cdot),\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}\mu_{r}\big)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\nabla_{x_{0}}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{r}^{0}+\nabla_{p}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Z_{r}^{0}+b_{r}\bigg]\mathrm{d}r+\sigma_{0}\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Z_{r}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{r}^{0},\quad\text{on $[t,T]$,}\\
&\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{t}^{0}=0,\quad\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}Y_{T}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T})\cdot\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}X_{T}^{0}+\big(\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T},\cdot),\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}\mu_{T}\big)+c_{T},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}a_{r}=&\bigg(\nabla_{px_{0}}^{2}H^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{px_{0}}^{2}H^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},\theta\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)Z_{r}^{0})d\theta\bigg)(\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0})\\
&+\bigg(\nabla_{pp}^{2}H^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{pp}^{2}H^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},\theta\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)Z_{r}^{0})d\theta\bigg)(\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}-Z_{r}^{0}),\\
b_{r}=&\bigg(\nabla_{x_{0}}f^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x_{0}}f^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},\theta\hat{\mu}_{r}+(1-\theta)\mu_{r})\mathrm{d}\theta\bigg)\cdot(\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0})\\
&+\bigg(\delta_{\mu}f^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r},\cdot)-\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{\mu}f^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},\theta\hat{\mu}_{r}+(1-\theta)\mu_{r},\cdot)\mathrm{d}\theta,\hat{\mu}_{r}-\mu_{r}\bigg)\\
&-\bigg(\nabla_{x_{0}}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x_{0}}\hat{L}^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},\theta\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)Z_{r}^{0})d\theta\bigg)\cdot(\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0})\\
&-\bigg(\nabla_{p}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{p}\hat{L}^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},\theta\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)Z_{r}^{0})d\theta\bigg)\cdot(\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}-Z_{r}^{0})\end{split}

and

\begin{split}c_{T}=&-\bigg(\nabla_{x_{0}}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x_{0}}g^{0}(\theta\hat{X}_{T}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{T}^{0},\theta\hat{\mu}_{T}+(1-\theta)\mu_{T})\mathrm{d}\theta\bigg)\cdot(\hat{X}_{T}^{0}-X_{T}^{0})\\
&-\bigg(\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T},\cdot)-\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{\mu}g^{0}(\theta X_{T}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{T}^{0},\theta\hat{\mu}_{T}+(1-\theta)\mu_{T},\cdot),\hat{\mu}_{T}-\mu_{T}\bigg).\end{split}

We then notice that
(\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{\mu}},\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{u}},\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}{\bm{m}})
satisfies, on
[t,T]
,

\begin{split}&\partial_{r}\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}\mu_{r}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}\mu_{r}-{\rm div}_{x}\big(\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\triangle^{\hskip-1.0pt2}\mu_{r}+\mu_{r}\nabla_{pp}^{2}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\nabla_{x}\delta^{2}u_{r}(x)\big)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\rm div}_{x}\big(d_{r}(x)\big)=0,\quad\text{on }\mathbb{T}^{d},\\
&\mathrm{d}_{r}\delta^{2}u_{r}(x)=\Big[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta^{2}u_{r}(x)+\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta^{2}u_{r}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r})\cdot\delta^{2}X_{r}^{0}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r},\cdot),\delta^{2}\mu_{r}\Big)+j_{r}(x)\Big]\mathrm{d}r+\mathrm{d}_{r}\delta^{2}m_{r}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\\
&\delta\mu_{t}=0,\quad\delta u_{T}(x)=\nabla_{x_{0}}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})\cdot\delta^{2}X_{T}^{0}+\Big(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T},\cdot),\delta^{2}\mu_{T}\Big)+k_{T},\quad x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}d_{r}(x)=&\big(\hat{\mu}_{r}-\mu_{r}\big)\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{pp}^{2}\hat{H}(x,\theta\nabla_{x}\hat{u}_{r}(x)+(1-\theta)\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\mathrm{d}\theta\bigg)\big(\nabla_{x}\hat{u}_{r}(x)-\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x)\big)\\
&+\mu_{r}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\theta\nabla_{ppp}^{3}\hat{H}\big(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x)+\tilde{\theta}\theta(\nabla_{x}\hat{u}_{r}(x)-\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\big)\big(\nabla_{x}\hat{u}_{r}(x)-\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x)\big)^{\otimes 2}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta,\\
j_{r}(x)=&-\bigg(\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\big(x,\theta\nabla_{x}\hat{u}_{r}(x)+(1-\theta)\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x)\big)\mathrm{d}\theta\bigg)\cdot\big(\nabla_{x}\hat{u}_{r}(x)-\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x)\big)\\
&+\bigg(\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}\big(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},x,\theta\hat{\mu}_{r}+(1-\theta)\mu_{r}\big)\mathrm{d}\theta\bigg)\cdot(\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0})\\
&+\bigg(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r},\cdot)-\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{\mu}f_{r}\big(\theta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{r}^{0},x,\theta\hat{\mu}_{r}+(1-\theta)\mu_{r},\cdot\big)\mathrm{d}\theta,\hat{\mu}_{r}-\mu_{r}\bigg),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}k_{T}(x)=&-\bigg(\nabla_{x_{0}}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{x_{0}}g\big(\theta\hat{X}_{T}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{T}^{0},x,\theta\hat{\mu}_{T}+(1-\theta)\mu_{T}\big)\mathrm{d}\theta\bigg)\cdot(\hat{X}_{T}^{0}-X_{T}^{0})\\
&-\bigg(\delta_{\mu}g(X_{T}^{0},x,\mu_{T})-\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{\mu}g(\theta X_{T}^{0}+(1-\theta)X_{T}^{0},x,\theta\hat{\mu}_{T}+(1-\theta)\mu_{T},\cdot),\hat{\mu}_{T}-\mu_{T}\bigg).\end{split}

Next, we recall

{\bm{\mu}}

and

\hat{\bm{\mu}}

take values in the space
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.
We
deduce that

\begin{split}&\|d_{r}\|_{-1}\leq C\Bigl(\|\hat{u}_{r}-u_{r}\|_{2}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{t},\mu_{t})+\|\hat{u}_{r}-u_{r}\|_{1}^{2}\Bigr),\quad r\in[t,T]\\
&\|k_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}\leq C\Bigl(|\hat{X}_{T}^{0}-X_{T}^{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{T},\mu_{T})^{2}\Bigr),\\
&\|j_{r}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}\leq C\Bigl(\|\hat{u}_{r}-u_{r}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{2}+|\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{r},\mu_{r})^{2}\Bigr),\\
&\int_{t}^{T}|a_{r}|dr\leq C\bigg[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}|\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0}|^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}|\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}-Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\bigg]\\
&\int_{t}^{T}|b_{r}|dr\leq C\bigg[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}|\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0}|^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu}_{r},\mu_{r})^{2}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{t}^{T}|\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}-Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\bigg],\\
&|c_{T}|\leq C\Big(|\hat{X}_{T}^{0}-X_{T}^{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\hat{\mu}_{T},\mu_{T})\Big).\end{split}

We further apply (
5.5
)
to obtain,
for any

p\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\|d_{r}\|_{-{\mathbb{r}}+2}^{p}\Big]\leq C_{p}\big(\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2p}(\hat{\mu},\mu)+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2p}\big),\\
&\mathbb{E}\Big[\|k_{T}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{p}\Big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2p}(\hat{\mu},\mu)+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2p}\big),\\
&\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\|j_{r}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-1}^{p}\Big]\leq C\big(\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2p}(\hat{\mu},\mu)+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2p}\big),\\
&\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{t}^{T}|a_{r}|dr\bigg)^{p}+\bigg(\int_{t}^{T}|b_{r}|dr\bigg)^{p}+c_{T}^{2}\bigg]\leq C\big(\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2p}(\hat{\mu},\mu)+|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2p}\big).\end{split}

Therefore, by Theorem
5.2
, we have (
5.40
).
∎

and

in Assumption

,

,
such that, for

(ii)
and

in the definition of

In the following statement, we address the spatial regularity of the

and

:

Proposition 5.10
.

Given two 4-tuples
(t,x_{0},\mu,y),(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu},\hat{y})\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, a
unitary vector
e\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, two scalars
\vartheta_{0},\vartheta\in[0,1]
, a
fixed
\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})

and
a
d
-tuple
l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1\}^{d}
with
|l|:=\sum_{i=1}^{d}l_{i}\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
,
call

({\bm{\mu}},{\bm{u}},{\bm{m}},{\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{Y}}^{0},{\bm{Z}}^{0})

and
(\hat{\bm{\mu}},\hat{\bm{u}},\hat{\bm{m}},\hat{\bm{X}}^{0},\hat{\bm{Y}}^{0},\hat{\bm{Z}}^{0})
the solutions of the system (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) with
(t,x_{0},\mu)
and
(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})
as
respective initial conditions and then
(\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta{\bm{u}},\delta{\bm{m}},\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})
and
(\delta\hat{\bm{\mu}},\delta\hat{\bm{u}},\delta\hat{\bm{m}},\delta\hat{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta\hat{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta\hat{\bm{Z}}^{0})
the solutions of the
linearized system (
5.32
) with
(t,\vartheta_{0}e,\vartheta(-1)^{|l|}\nabla^{l}\delta_{y})
and
(t,\vartheta_{0}e,\vartheta(-1)^{|l|}\nabla^{l}\delta_{\hat{y}})
as respective initial conditions.

Then,
for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
for some
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
only depending on the parameters

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}
in Assumption
(
Â
)
and on

{\mathbb{r}}
, we can find a constant
C
, only depending
on the parameters in Assumption
(
Â
)
and on
{\mathbb{r}}
,
such that, for any

p\in[1,4]
,

(5.41)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Bigl(\|\delta\hat{\mu}_{r}-\delta\mu_{r}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{2p}+\|\delta\hat{u}_{r}-\delta u_{r}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}^{2p}+\|\delta\hat{m}_{r}-\delta m_{r}\|_{{\mathbb{s}}-2}^{2p}\Bigr)\biggr]\\
&+\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Bigl(|\delta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-\delta X_{r}^{0}|^{2p}+|\delta\hat{Y}_{t}^{0}-\delta Y_{t}^{0}|^{2p}\Bigr)+\biggl(\int_{t}^{T}|\delta\hat{Z}_{r}^{0}-\delta Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr)^{p}\bigg]\\
&\leq C\Big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2p}(\hat{\mu},\mu)+|\hat{y}-y|^{2p(\mathbb{r}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)}\Big).\end{split}

In particular,
for any
y,\hat{y}\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
,
for any
k\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor\mathbb{s}\rfloor-1\}
,

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\cdot,\hat{\mu})\big\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}+\big|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})\big|\leq C\Big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu},\mu)\Big),

(5.42)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{y}^{k}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{k}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\cdot,\hat{\mu},\hat{y})\big\|_{{\mathbb{s}}}+\big|\nabla_{y}^{k}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{k}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu},\hat{y})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|+\mathbb{W}_{1}(\hat{\mu},\mu)+|\hat{y}-y|^{\mathbb{r}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor}\Big),

and, for any
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(C,{\mathbb{r}}-1)

and
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}(C,{\mathbb{r}}-1,{\mathbb{s}})
.

Applying Theorem
5.1
, we obtain
(for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
)

(5.43)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\bigg[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Big(\|\hat{u}_{r}-u_{r}\|_{s}^{4p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{4p}(\hat{\mu}_{r},\mu_{r})+|\hat{X}_{r}^{0}-X_{r}^{0}|^{4p}+|\hat{Y}_{r}^{0}-Y_{r}^{0}|^{4p}\Bigr)+\biggl(\int_{t}^{T}|\hat{Z}^{0}_{r}-Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr)^{2p}\bigg]\\
&\leq C\Big[|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{4p}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{4p}(\hat{\mu},\mu)\Big].\end{split}

By Theorem
5.5
,

(5.44)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{r\in[t,T]}\Bigl(\|\delta\mu_{r}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{4p}+\|\delta\hat{\mu}_{r}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}^{4p}+\|\delta u_{r}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{4p}+\|\delta\hat{u}_{r}\|_{\mathbb{s}}^{4p}+|\delta X_{r}^{0}|^{4p}+|\delta\hat{X}_{r}^{0}|^{4p}+|\delta Y_{r}^{0}|^{4p}+|\delta\hat{Y}_{r}^{0}|^{4p}\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}\bigl(|\delta Z_{t}^{0}|^{2}+|\delta\hat{Z}_{t}^{0}|^{2}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr)^{2p}\biggr]\\
&\leq C.\end{split}

It remains to see that, since
|l|\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
, we have

\|\nabla^{l}\delta_{\hat{y}}-\nabla^{l}\delta_{y}\|_{-\mathbb{r}+1}\leq|\hat{y}-y|^{\mathbb{r}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor}.

In order to conclude, we follow the proof of Proposition
5.8
.
The idea is to write

(\delta\hat{\bm{\mu}}-\delta{\bm{\mu}},\delta\hat{\bm{u}}-\delta{\bm{u}},\delta\hat{\bm{m}}-\delta{\bm{m}},\delta\hat{\bm{X}}^{0}-\delta{\bm{X}}^{0},\delta\hat{\bm{Y}}^{0}-\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0},\delta\hat{\bm{Z}}^{0}-\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0})

as a solution of a linear system of the form
(
5.15
), with suitable coefficients

(a_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,

(b_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,

c_{T}
,
(d_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,

(j_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

and
k_{T}
.
Briefly, all these terms can be bounded from above by
products of two terms: one of the norms appearing in
(
5.43
)
and one of the norms
of
(
5.44
). Cauchy-Schwarz inequality permits to handle
the
2p
moment of any of these products. By (
5.43
) and
(
5.44
), we obtain (
5.41
).

Then, we get the first line in
(
5.42
)
by choosing
p=1
,
\vartheta_{0}=1
,
\vartheta=0
and
y=\hat{y}
. The second inequality is
obtained by choosing

p=1
,
\vartheta_{0}=0
and
\vartheta=1
.
∎

5.5
Regularity in time of the derivatives

We now address the time continuity of the derivatives. We start with the following statement:

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
{\mathbb{r}}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})

and let Assumption
(
Â
)
be in force.
Then, there exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on
d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
and on
{\mathbb{r}}
, such that, for

T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
, for any
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, it holds

(5.45)

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\to 0}\Big(\big\|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}}+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r},\mathbb{r}-1}\Bigr)=0,\end{split}

where

\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{r}},{\mathbb{r}}-1}
denotes the standard Hölder norm
2
2

2

Let
r,s>0
with
r,s\not\in\mathbb{N}
. If a function
h:(x,y)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\mapsto h(x,y)
has
mixed derivatives up to the order
\lfloor r\rfloor
in
x
and
\lfloor s\rfloor
in
y
, we set

\|g\|_{\lfloor r\rfloor,\lfloor s\rfloor}:=\sup_{k=0,\cdots,\lfloor r\rfloor}\sup_{l=0,\cdots\lfloor s\rfloor}\sup_{x,y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\nabla_{x}^{k}\nabla_{y}^{l}g(x,y)|,

and, if
\nabla_{x}^{\lfloor r\rfloor}\nabla_{y}^{\lfloor s\rfloor}h
is
(r-\lfloor r\rfloor,s-\lfloor s\rfloor)
-Hölder continuous, we also set

\|g\|_{(r,s)}:=\|g\|_{\lfloor r\rfloor,\lfloor s\rfloor}+\sup_{(x,y),(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}:(x,y)\not=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}\frac{|\nabla_{x}^{\lfloor r\rfloor}\nabla_{y}^{\lfloor s\rfloor}g(x,y)-\nabla_{x}^{\lfloor r\rfloor}\nabla_{y}^{\lfloor s\rfloor}g(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})|}{|x-x^{\prime}|^{r-\lfloor r\rfloor}+|y-y^{\prime}|^{s-\lfloor s\rfloor}}.

on
the space
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{r}},{\mathbb{r}}-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d})
of functions
h:(x,y)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\mapsto h(x,y)
that have crossed derivatives in
(x,y)
up the order
\lfloor{\mathbb{r}}\rfloor
in
x
and
\lfloor{\mathbb{r}}\rfloor-1
in
y
,
all the derivatives being

{\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor

Hölder continuous in
(x,y)
. Similarly,

(5.46)

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\to 0}\Big(\big|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\bigr|+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}-1}\Big)=0.\end{split}

Given
(x_{0},\mu),(\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we can derive from Proposition
5.8
that, for

T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
and for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(5.47)

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\cdot,\hat{\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\\
&=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big)+O\big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\hat{\mu},\mu)\big),\end{split}

where the equality holds true in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{s}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and the Landau notation
O(\cdot)
is uniform in
(t,x_{0},\mu)
, and

(5.48)

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\\
&=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big)+O\big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\hat{\mu},\mu)\big).\end{split}

We now fix
(x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

and let
\mathbb{u}:=({\mathbb{r}}+{\mathbb{s}})/2
.
Recall (
5.35
) and the fact that
\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)=K^{0,x_{0}}(t,x_{0},\mu)
. Therefore, the
collection
\{\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu),\ t\in[0,T]\}

is bounded and thus relatively compact in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By Proposition
5.10

(applied with the indices
({\mathbb{u}},{\mathbb{s}})
in lieu of
({\mathbb{r}},{\mathbb{s}})
)
and under the same generic condition
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
as therein,
the collection of functions

\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu),t\in[0,T]\}

is relatively compact in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{r}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
,
the collection of functions
\{y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y),t\in[0,T]\}

is relatively compact in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{r}-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, and
the collection of functions

\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y),t\in[0,T]\}

is relatively compact in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{r},{\mathbb{r}}-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})
. Any limits
\Phi^{0,x_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\Phi^{x_{0}},\Phi^{0,\mu}:\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
\Phi^{\mu}:\mathbb{\mathbb{}}T^{d}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
obtained by letting
t
tend to some
t_{0}\in[0,T]
in (
5.47
) and (
5.48
) must satisfy

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},\hat{x}_{0},\cdot,\hat{\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\\
&=\Phi^{x_{0}}(\cdot)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\Phi^{\mu}(\cdot,y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big)+O\big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\hat{\mu},\mu)\big),\end{split}

where the equality holds true in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathbb{r}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, and

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},\hat{x}_{0},\hat{\mu})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)\\
&=\Phi^{0,x_{0}}\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\Phi^{0,\mu}(y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big)+O\big(|\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0}|^{2}+\mathbb{W}_{1}^{2}(\hat{\mu},\mu)\big).\end{split}

The last two displays show that

\begin{split}&\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},x,\mu)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},x,\mu,y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big)\\
&=\Phi^{x_{0}}(x)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\Phi^{\mu}(x,y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big),\quad x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big)\\
=&\Phi^{0,x_{0}}\cdot(\hat{x}_{0}-x_{0})+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\Phi^{0,\mu}(y)\big(\hat{\mu}(\mathrm{d}y)-\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\big),\end{split}

which implies that

\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},x,\mu)=\Phi^{x_{0}}(x),\quad\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},x,\mu,y)=\Phi^{\mu}(x,y),

and

\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)=\Phi^{0,x_{0}},\quad\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu,y)=\Phi^{0,\mu}(y).

This proves (
5.45
) and (
5.46
).
∎

We now refine Proposition

5.11
. (using the same notation as therein for the norm

\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{r}},{\mathbb{r}}-1}
,
see footnote

2
):

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
\mathbb{r}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
.
Under Assumption
(
Â
)
, there
exist a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on the parameters

d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
and on
{\mathbb{r}}
, and a constant

C>0
, only depending on
the parameters in
Assumption
(
Â
)

and on
{\mathbb{r}}
,
such that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
for all
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})

and
h>0
such that
t+h\leq T
,

(5.49)

\begin{split}&\big\|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}}+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r},\mathbb{r}-1}\\
&\quad\leq Ch^{1/2},\end{split}

and

(5.50)

\begin{split}&\big|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\big|+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}-1}\leq Ch^{1/2}.\end{split}

In fact, one could have directly established Proposition

5.12
, but
we felt better to start with Proposition

5.11
, because we use repeatedly the two
properties (
5.45
) and (
5.46
)
in the rest of the analysis.

We start with the proof of the second inequality in (
5.49
). To do so, we recall
from Lemma

5.6
and
Proposition
5.8
that

\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)=K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)
,
for
y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
and for

l\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1\}^{d}
with

|l|\leq\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor-1
,
with

K^{x_{0}}

and

K^{\mu}_{l}
being defined in
(
5.34
). By the latter, we have
(with the same notation as therein and under the generic notation
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
)

K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)-K_{l}^{\mu}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y)=\delta u_{t}^{l,y}(x)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y),

and then, by recalling
(
5.32
),

(5.51)

\begin{split}&K_{l}^{\mu}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)-K_{l}^{\mu}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\\
&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[\delta u_{t+h}^{l,y}(x)\bigr]-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{t}^{t+h}\Big[-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}\delta u_{r}^{l,y}(x)+\nabla_{p}\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{r}(x))\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta u_{r}^{l,y}(x)-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r})\cdot\delta X_{r}^{0,l,y}\\
&\hskip 45.0pt-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}(X_{r}^{0},x,\mu_{r}),\delta\mu_{r}^{l,y}\Big)\Big]\mathrm{d}r.\end{split}

Again, it is worth stressing that the pair of triples

(\delta{\bm{X}}^{0,l,y},\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0,l,y},\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0,l,y})
and

(\delta{\bm{\mu}}^{l,y},\delta{\bm{u}}^{l,y},\delta{\bm{m}}^{l,y})
solve
the linearized system
(
5.32
)
with
\triangle\mu=(-1)^{|l|}\nabla^{l}\delta_{y}\in\mathcal{C}^{1-\mathbb{r}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

(for the same

\mathbb{r}
as in the statement) and
\triangle x^{0}=0
as initial conditions at
time
t
, when
{\bm{X}}^{0}

starts from
x_{0}
at time
t
and

{\bm{\mu}}
from
\mu
.
Moreover,
using (
5.33
),
we can bound the

L^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})
-norms
of all the
terms entering the integrand
of
(
5.51
).
As a result,

(5.52)

\begin{split}&\Bigl|\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\Bigr|\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\Bigl|\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h},y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\Bigr|\Bigr]+Ch.\end{split}

Using
the forward equation in
(
5.1
), we have

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[|X_{t+h}^{0}-x_{0}|^{2}\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[|X_{t+h}^{0}-X_{t}^{0}|^{2}\bigr]\leq Ch{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{t}^{t+h}\bigl(1+|Z_{s}^{0}|^{2}\bigr)\mathrm{d}s.

By (
5.3
), the right-hand
side above is bounded by
Ch
for a possibly new value of
C
.

Also, using
the forward equation in
(
5.2
)
together with the bound (
5.4
), we have

{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{t+h},\mu_{t})\leq Ch
. And then, using the Lipschitz regularity of

\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}
in the variables
x_{0}
and
\mu

(see
(
5.42
)),
we obtain

(5.53)

\begin{split}&\Bigl|\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\Bigr|\leq Ch^{1/2},\end{split}

which proves the second inequality in
(
5.49
).

We prove
the second inequality in
a similar manner, using the representation

\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)=K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)=\delta Y_{t}^{0,l,y}

(with the same definition as above for

\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0,l,y}
).
We have

\begin{split}&\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,y)\\
&=K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)-K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,y)\\
&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[\delta Y_{t+h}^{0,l,y}\bigr]-K_{l}^{0,\mu}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\int_{t}^{t+h}\Big[\nabla_{x_{0}}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\cdot\delta X_{r}^{0}+\nabla_{p}\hat{L}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},Z_{r}^{0})\cdot\delta Z_{r}^{0}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt-\nabla_{x_{0}}f_{r}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r})\cdot\delta X_{r}^{0}-\Big(\delta_{\mu}f_{r}^{0}(X_{r}^{0},\mu_{r}),\delta\mu_{r}\Big)\Big]\mathrm{d}r.\end{split}

This is the same as before except for
the fact that the bound for
the term
\delta Z_{r}^{0}
in
(
5.33
) is weaker than the bound satisfied by the other terms therein.
Here is how we remedy this difficulty: it suffices to say (thanks
to Cauchy-Schwarz inequality) that

{\mathbb{E}}\int_{t}^{t+h}|\delta Z_{r}^{0}|\mathrm{d}r\leq Ch^{1/2}
. Proceeding as in the derivation of
(
5.52
), we deduce

(5.54)

\begin{split}&\Bigl|\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,y)\Bigr|\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\Bigl|\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h},y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,y)\Bigr|\Bigr]+Ch^{1/2}.\end{split}

And then, following the proof of
(
5.53
), we get

(5.55)

\begin{split}&\Bigl|\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)-\nabla_{y}^{l}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,y)\Bigr|\leq Ch^{1/2}.\end{split}

This completes the proof.
∎

5.6
Identification of the martingale representation term

The next step is to identify the process
{\bm{Z}}^{0}
in
(
2.11
):

Proposition 5.13
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
,
there exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on the parameters

d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
,
such that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
the process
{\bm{Z}}^{0}
in
(
5.1
) admits the following representation:

\forall t\in[0,T],\quad{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\{Z_{t}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\}\bigr)=1,

and this, for any choice of initial condition
(x_{0},\mu)
in
(
5.1
).

The result is in fact a consequence of the more general
representation property (the reader can skip the demonstration on first reading):

Lemma 5.14
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
,
consider
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
as in Theorem

5.1
. Then, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
and

1.

for
any initial condition

(x_{0},\mu)
in
(
5.1
),

2.

for any continuous
function
{\mathcal{V}}^{0}:[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

with the property that, for some
C_{0}>0
and some
{\mathbb{r}}\in(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor,{\mathbb{s}})
,

{\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(C_{0},{\mathbb{r}}-1)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, and

\begin{split}&\forall(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),\\
&\quad\lim_{h\to 0}\Big(\big|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\bigr|+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}-1}\Big)=0,\end{split}

3.

for any
triplet
({\bm{U}}^{0},{\bm{V}}^{0},{\bm{\ell}}^{0})

in
{\mathscr{S}}^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};{\mathbb{R}})\times{\mathscr{H}}^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};{\mathbb{R}})\times{\mathscr{H}}^{2}(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};{\mathbb{R}})

satisfying

(5.56)

\mathrm{d}U^{0}_{t}=\ell^{0}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}V^{0}_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad U_{T}^{0}={\mathcal{V}}^{0}(T,X_{T}^{0},\mu_{T}),

and,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,
U_{t}^{0}={\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})
,

the process
{\bm{V}}^{0}
admits the following representation:

\forall t\in[0,T],\quad{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\{V_{t}^{0}=\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\}\bigr)=1.

The proof is standard. Here, we take it from

[
19
, Lemma 4.11]
. We consider a uniform subdivision

{\bm{\tau}}:=(t_{i})_{i=0,\cdots,n}
with
0=:t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}:=T
of stepsize
h
together with a simple

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
-valued
adapted
process

{\bm{\pi}}=(\pi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
of the form

(5.57)

\pi_{t}=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\pi^{(i)}{\mathbf{1}}_{(t_{i},t_{i+1}]}(t),\quad t\in[0,T],

where, for some

K\geq 0
and for each

i=0,\cdots,n-1
,
\pi^{(i)}
belongs to
L^{\infty}(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{t_{i}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0};{\mathbb{R}}^{d})

with

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}(\{|\pi^{(i)}|\leq K\})=1
.
For
i=0,\cdots,n-1
, we then have

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\times U_{t_{i+1}}^{0}\biggr]={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\bigl(\pi^{(i)}\cdot(B^{0}_{t_{i+1}}-B^{0}_{t_{i}})\bigr)\times U_{t_{i+1}}^{0}\Bigr].\end{split}

First Step.
Using the backward equation
(
5.56
),
it is standard to prove that
there exists a constant
C

(only depending on
{\bm{\pi}}
through
K
)
such that, for all
i=0,\cdots,n-1
,

(5.58)

\begin{split}&\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\times U_{t_{i+1}}^{0}\biggr]-\sigma_{0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot V_{t}^{0}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\biggr|\\
&=\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\times\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\ell_{t}^{0}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]\biggr|\\
&\leq(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}|\pi_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}|\ell_{t}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\biggr]^{1/2}\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\biggl\{\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[|\ell_{t}^{0}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}t\biggr\}^{1/2}\leq Ch\epsilon(h),\end{split}

where we have let

(5.59)

\epsilon(h):=\biggl\{h+\sup_{0\leq s<t<T:|t-s|\leq h}\int_{s}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[|Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}+|\ell_{r}^{0}|^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}r\biggr\}^{1/2},\quad h>0.

Because
\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}[|Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}+|\ell_{r}^{0}|^{2}]\mathrm{d}r<\infty
, we have

\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\epsilon(h)=0
.

Now, we observe that the left-hand side
in the top line of
(
5.58
)
is equal to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\times U_{t_{i+1}}^{0}\biggr]

(5.60)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\times\Bigl({\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i+1}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i+1}})-{\mathcal{V}}^{0}(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}})\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\times\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}\biggl[\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}}\Bigr)\cdot\bigl(X_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-X_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}},y\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\bigl(\mu_{t_{i+1}}-\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)(y)\biggr]\mathrm{d}r\biggr)\biggr].

Second Step.
