We first handle the term on the last line of
(
5.60
).
Using the forward equation in
(
5.2
), we have, for any

r\in[0,1]
,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}},y\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\bigl(\mu_{t_{i+1}}-\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)(y)\\
&=\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}},y\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}(y)ds\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}},y\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}u_{s}(y)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}(y)ds.\end{split}

By item 2 in the statement,
the two functions

\Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}

and

\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}

are bounded. Therefore,
we can modify the value of

C
such that, for all

i=0,\cdots,n-1
, with probability 1 under

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,

(5.61)

\begin{split}\biggl|\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}},y\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\bigl(\mu_{t_{i+1}}-\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)(y)\biggr|\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i}).\end{split}

And then, by Cauchy-Schwarz’ inequality (and recalling
(
5.59
)),

(5.62)

\begin{split}&\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{t}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}\biggr)\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\times\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}\biggl[\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}},y\Bigr)\mathrm{d}\bigl(\mu_{t_{i+1}}-\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)(y)\biggr]\mathrm{d}r\biggr)\biggr]\biggr|\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{3/2}\leq Ch\epsilon(h).\end{split}

Third Step.
We now address
the term on the third line of (
5.60
).
Proceeding as
in the derivation of
(
5.61
),
we have, for all
i=0,\cdots,n-1
,
with probability 1 under

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
,

{\mathbb{W}}_{1}\bigl(\mu_{t_{i}},\mu_{t_{i+1}}\bigr)\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2}.

Therefore, thanks to the boundedness and Lipschitz regularity of

\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}
(see
item 2 in the statement), we get
(recalling again
(
5.59
))

(5.63)

\begin{split}&\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{1}\biggl[\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}}\Bigr)\cdot\bigl(X_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-X_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr)\biggr]dr\\
&\hskip 30.0pt-\sigma_{0}\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}}\Bigr)\cdot\bigl(B_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-B_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr)\biggr|^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2} \epsilon(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sigma_{0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl|\int_{0}^{1}\biggl[\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},rX_{t_{i+1}}^{0}+(1-r)X_{t_{i}}^{0},r\mu_{t_{i+1}}+(1-r)\mu_{t_{i}}\Bigr)\cdot\bigl(B_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-B_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr)\biggr]\mathrm{d}r\\
&\hskip 30.0pt-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\Bigl(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}}\Bigr)\cdot\bigl(B_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-B_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr)\biggr|^{2}\biggr]^{1/2}\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2} \epsilon(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\Bigl\{1\wedge\Bigl(\bigl|X_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-X_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr|^{2}+{\mathbb{W}}_{1}\bigl(\mu_{t_{i}},\mu_{t_{i+1}}\bigr)^{2}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\bigl|B_{t_{i+1}}^{0}-B_{t_{i}}^{0}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr]^{1/2}\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2} \epsilon(t_{i+1}-t_{i})+C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[1\wedge\biggl((t_{i+1}-t_{i})\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}|Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr)^{2}\biggr]^{1/4}\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2} \epsilon(t_{i+1}-t_{i})+C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[1\wedge\biggl((t_{i+1}-t_{i})\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}|Z_{r}^{0}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\biggr)\biggr]^{1/4}\\
&\leq C(t_{i+1}-t_{i})^{1/2} \epsilon(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\leq Ch^{1/2}\epsilon(h),\end{split}

with the last line following from Young’s inequality.

Fourth Step.

Finally, by combining
(
5.58
),(
5.60
),
(
5.62
)
and
(
5.63
).

\begin{split}&\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{s}\cdot\Bigl(\sigma_{0}V_{s}^{0}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\pi_{s}\cdot\Bigl(\sigma_{0}\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\bigl(t_{i+1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\biggr|\leq Ch\epsilon(h),\end{split}

which yields

(5.64)

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\biggl|{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\pi_{s}\cdot\Bigl(\sigma_{0}V_{s}^{0}\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{T}\pi_{s}\cdot\Bigl(\sigma_{0}\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\bigl(t^{+}({\bm{\tau}},s),X_{t^{-}({\bm{\tau}},s)}^{0},\mu_{t^{-}({\bm{\tau}},s)}\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\biggr|\leq C\epsilon(h),\end{split}

with

t^{+}({\bm{\tau}},s):=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}t_{i+1}{\mathbf{1}}_{(t_{i},t_{i+1}]}(s),\quad t^{-}({\bm{\tau}},s):=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}t_{i}{\mathbf{1}}_{[t_{i},t_{i+1})}(s),\quad s\in[0,T].

We observe that, as the stepsize
h
of
{\bm{\tau}}
tends to
0
,

t^{+}({\bm{\tau}},s)
and

t^{-}({\bm{\tau}},s)
tend to
s
.

By boundedness and continuity of
\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}

(see item 2) and by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem,
it is straightforward to prove that

(5.65)

\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\bigl(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s}\bigr)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\bigl(t^{+}({\bm{\tau}},s),X_{t^{-}({\bm{\tau}},s)}^{0},\mu_{t^{-}({\bm{\tau}},s)}\bigr)\Bigr|\mathrm{d}s\biggr]=0.

It remains to choose
{\bm{\pi}}
as a bounded simple process
(as in
(
5.57
))
but with respect to a fixed partition

{\bm{\tau}}^{0}:=(t_{i}^{0})_{i=0,\cdots,n_{0}}
,
with
0=:t_{0}^{0}<t_{1}^{0}<\cdots<t_{n_{0}}:=T
.
The stepsize of
{\bm{\tau}}^{0}
is denoted
h_{0}
and is kept fixed.
We then refine the partition
{\bm{\tau}}^{0}
considering
another partition
{\bm{\tau}}\supset{\bm{\tau}}^{0}
.
The stepsize of
{\bm{\tau}}
is denoted
h
and is sent to zero
in the next lines.
The main observation is that
{\bm{\pi}}

is also a bounded simple process with respect to the partition

{\bm{\tau}}
. This makes it possible to apply (
5.64
)
and
(
5.65
), from which we deduce
(sending
h
to
0
) that

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\pi_{t}\cdot\Bigl(\sigma_{0}\Bigl[V_{t}^{0}-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\bigl(t,X_{t},\mu_{t}\bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t\biggr]=0.

The above is true for any bounded simple process
{\bm{\pi}}
. By a density argument,
it is true for any bounded progressively measurable process, from which we deduce that,
for
{\rm Leb}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost every
(t,\omega^{0})
,

\sigma_{0}\Bigl[V_{t}^{0}-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{V}}^{0}\bigl(t,X_{t},\mu_{t}\bigr)\Bigr]=0.

This completes the proof.
∎

5.7
Derivation of the master equations

We now address the two PDEs (called master equations)
that are satisfied by
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
.
The derivation of the master equation for
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
relies on the following formula (which is very much in the spirit of a Kolmogorov formula):

Proposition 5.15
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
, there
exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on the parameters

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
such that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
we have the following representation of

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)
, for any
(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
:

(5.66)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[g^{0}(\tilde{X}_{T}^{0},\mu)\bigr]-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}\bigl(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}\bigl(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\Bigl(f_{s}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr],\end{split}

where
\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0}=(\tilde{X}^{0}_{t})_{t_{0}\leq t\leq T}
solves the
SDE

(5.67)

\mathrm{d}\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}=-\nabla_{p}H^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[t_{0},T];\quad\tilde{X}_{t_{0}}^{0}=x_{0}.

Pay attention that
\mu
is fixed in
(
5.67
)
(equivalently,
(
5.67
) is not a McKean-Vlasov equation).

Throughout the proof,
T
is implicitly taken less than
{\mathfrak{C}}
so that all the results already proved in this section can be freely applied.

For simplicity, we establish the representation at time
t_{0}=0
.
Thanks to the regularity of
\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
(see
Proposition

5.10
)
the equation
(
5.67
)
has a unique solution.

First Step.

We fix
t\in[0,T]
. With
\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0}
as in the statement, we consider
the solution

({\bm{X}}^{0},{\bm{\mu}})=(X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})_{t\leq s\leq T}

to the two forward equations in
(
5.1
)
and
(
5.2
)
with
X^{0}_{t}=\tilde{X}^{0}_{t}
and
\mu_{t}=\mu
as initial condition
at time
t
.
By
Proposition

5.13
, the equation for

{\bm{X}}^{0}
can be rewritten as

(5.68)

\mathrm{d}X_{s}^{0}=-\nabla_{p}H^{0}\bigl(X_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})\bigr)\mathrm{d}s+\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{0},\quad s\in[t,T];\quad X_{t}^{0}=\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}.

In particular, by Lipschitz regularity of
\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}

and from the identity
\mu_{t}=\mu
,
we obtain, for a fixed (small)
h>0
,

(5.69)

\begin{split}\sup_{t\leq s\leq t+h}|\tilde{X}_{s}^{0}-X_{s}^{0}|&\leq C\int_{t}^{t+h}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s},\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}s\leq Ch^{3/2}.\end{split}

Now,
by
(
5.69
),

(5.70)

\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},\mu)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]\\
&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]\\
&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]+O(h^{3/2}),\end{split}

where

|O(h^{3/2})|\leq Ch^{3/2}
.

Second Step.

We handle the term on the last line of
(
5.70
). Using the regularity of

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
in the third argument
together with
the equation for
{\bm{\mu}}

in
(
5.2
), we have (the reader may compare the expansion below with
[
19
, Theorem 5.99]
):

\begin{split} &{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t})\\
&=\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{s},y)\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}(y)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{s},y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,X_{s}^{0},y,\mu_{s})\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}(y)\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

And then, using
(
5.69
)
together with
the Lipschitz regularity
of
\Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
,
\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}

in the variables
x_{0}
and
\mu
and the Hölder regularity of the same functions in
y
(see (
5.42
)), we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})-{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\Bigr]

(5.71)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu_{t},y)\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu_{t},y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu_{t})\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{1+({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).

We then handle the term on the penultimate line of
(
5.70
).
Using the
backward equation in (
5.1
),
we have

(5.72)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[Y_{t+h}^{0}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]\\
&=Y_{t}^{0}-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(f_{s}^{0}(X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(X_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&={\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(f_{s}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu_{t})+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu_{t})\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]+O(h^{3/2}).\end{split}

Third Step.

We now insert
(
5.71
)
and
(
5.72
)
into
(
5.70
).
Recalling

X_{t}^{0}=\tilde{X}_{t}^{0}
and

\mu_{t}=\mu
,
we obtain

(5.73)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},\mu)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]={\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},\mu)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(f_{s}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{1+({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).\end{split}

We are now given a subdivision

{\bm{\tau}}=(t_{i})_{i=0,\cdots,n}

of
[0,T]
, with

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}=T

and
with stepsize
h
. Then, (
5.73
) says

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{i+1},\tilde{X}_{t_{i+1}}^{0},\mu)\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{i},\tilde{X}_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu)\bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{i+1},\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{i+1},\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\Bigl(f_{s}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{1+({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).\end{split}

Letting
t({\bm{\tau}},s):=t_{i+1}
if

s\in(t_{i},t_{i+1}]
, for
i=0,\dots,n-1
,
and summing over
i

the above expansion,
we get

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(0,x_{0},\mu)={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[g^{0}(\tilde{X}_{T}^{0},\mu)\bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}\bigl(t({\bm{\tau}},s),\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}\bigl(t({\bm{\tau}},s),\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(f_{s}^{0}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu)\bigr)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).\end{split}

Using the
boundedness and
time continuity of

\Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}

and

\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
(recall in particular
Proposition

5.12
) and observing
that
\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}t({\bm{\tau}},s)=s
, we can easily let
h
tend to
0
in the formula.
By Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, we get the expected formula.
∎

We have a similar result for
{\mathcal{U}}
:

Proposition 5.16
.

Under Assumption
(
Â
)
, there
exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on the parameters

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
such that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
the following representation of

{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu,x)
holds true, for any
(t_{0},x_{0},x,\mu)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
:

(5.74)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{0},x_{0},x,\mu)&={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[g(\tilde{X}_{T}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr]-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}\bigl(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}\bigl(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr)-f_{s}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr],\end{split}

where
\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0}=(\tilde{X}^{0}_{t})_{t_{0}\leq t\leq T}
solves the
SDE
(
5.67
).

The proof is similar to the proof of
Proposition

5.15
.
In particular, we just establish the representation at time
t_{0}=0
.

First Step.

For a fixed
t\in[0,T]
, we reuse the notation
(
5.68
)
and the bound
(
5.69
).
Following
(
5.70
),
we deduce from the Lipschitz regularity of

{\mathcal{U}}

(see
(
5.27
))
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]

(5.75)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t})-{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t})-{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]+O(h^{3/2}),

where

|O(h^{3/2})|\leq Ch^{3/2}
.

Second Step.

We follow the second step in the proof of Proposition

5.15
and
address the term on the last line of
(
5.75
). Using the regularity of

{\mathcal{U}}
in the third argument
(see Proposition

5.10
)
together with
the equation for
{\bm{\mu}}

in
(
5.2
), we have

\begin{split} &{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})-{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\\
&=\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{s},y)\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}(y)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{s},y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,X_{s}^{0},y,\mu_{s})\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}(y)\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

And then, using
(
5.69
)
together with
the Lipschitz regularity
of
\Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}
,
\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}
and
\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}

in the variables
x_{0}
and
\mu
and their Hölder regularity in
y
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})-{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\Bigr]

(5.76)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{t},y)\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{t},y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu_{t})\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{1+({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).

We then handle the term on the penultimate line of
(
5.75
).
Using the
backward equation in (
5.2
) (with the prescribed initial conditions for the two forward equations in (
5.1
) and (
5.2
)) and
(
5.27
)
again,
we have

(5.77)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t+h})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[u_{t+h}(x)\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]\\
&=u_{t}(x)+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}u_{s}(x)+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}u_{s}(x)\bigr)-f_{s}(X_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{s})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&={\mathcal{U}}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\\
&\hskip 10.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,X_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{s})+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,X_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{s})\bigr)-f_{s}(X_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{s})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&={\mathcal{U}}(t,X_{t}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\\
&\hskip 10.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{t})+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\bigr)-f_{s}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]+O(h^{3/2}).\end{split}

Third Step.

We now insert
(
5.76
)
and
(
5.77
)
into
(
5.75
).
We obtain

(5.78)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{t+h}^{0},x,\mu_{t})\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\bigr]={\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\tilde{X}_{t}^{0},x,\mu)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{t+h}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr)-f_{s}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+O\bigl(h^{1+({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).\end{split}

We are now given a subdivision

{\bm{\tau}}=(t_{i})_{i=0,\cdots,n}

of
[0,T]
, with

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}=T

and
with stepsize
h
. Then, (
5.78
) says

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t_{i+1},\tilde{X}_{t_{i+1}}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr]={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[{\mathcal{U}}(t_{i},\tilde{X}_{t_{i}}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{i+1},\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t_{i+1},\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr)-f_{s}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{1+({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).\end{split}

Letting
t({\bm{\tau}},s):=t_{i+1}
if

s\in(t_{i},t_{i+1}]
, for
i=0,\dots,n-1
,
and summing over
i

the above expansion, we get

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{U}}(0,x_{0},x,\mu)={\mathbb{E}}^{0}\bigl[g(\tilde{X}_{T}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}} \Delta_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t({\bm{\tau}},s),\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t({\bm{\tau}},s),\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{T}\Bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)+\hat{H}\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\bigr)-f_{s}(\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},x,\mu)\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]+O\bigl(h^{({\mathbb{r}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2}\bigr).\end{split}

Using the
boundedness and
time continuity
of all the derivatives
appearing above (see
Lemma

5.4

and
Proposition

5.12
)
and
observing
that
\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}t({\bm{\tau}},s)=s
, we can easily let
h
tend to
0
in the formula.
By Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, we get the expected formula.
∎

The following result makes the connection between Propositions
5.15

and

5.16

and the master equation.

Corollary 5.17
.

Let Assumption
(
Â
)

be in force. Assume also that:

i.

the coefficient
(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto f_{t}^{0}(x_{0},\mu)
is Hölder continuous in time, uniformly in

(x_{0},\mu)
; the coefficient
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto f_{t}(x_{0},x,\mu)
is Hölder continuous in time,
uniformly in
(x_{0},x,\mu)
;

ii.

x_{0}\mapsto g^{0}(x_{0},\mu)
has
Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
\mu
;
x_{0}\mapsto g(x_{0},x,\mu)

has Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
(x,\mu)
.

Then,
there
exists a constant
{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on the parameters

d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\kappa
,

\zeta
,
\sigma_{0}
and

\mathbb{s}

in Assumption
(
Â
)
such that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
the system of two master equations

(5.79)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}\Delta_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)-\nabla_{p}H^{0}\bigl(x_{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\bigr)\cdot\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\hat{L}^{0}\bigl(x_{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\bigr)+f_{t}^{0}(x_{0},\mu)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl\{\tfrac{1}{2}{\rm div}_{y}(\partial_{\mu}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y))-\partial_{\mu}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\bigr\}\mathrm{d}\mu(y)=0,\\
&V^{0}(T,x_{0},\mu)=g^{0}(x_{0},\mu),\end{split}

for
(t,x_{0},\mu)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, and

(5.80)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu)+\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu)+\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}\Delta_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu)-\hat{H}\bigl(x_{0},x,\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\bigr)+f_{t}(x_{0},x,\mu)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\nabla_{p}H^{0}\big(x_{0},\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\big)\cdot\nabla_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl\{\tfrac{1}{2}{\rm div}_{y}(\partial_{\mu}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y))-\partial_{\mu}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\cdot\nabla_{p}\hat{H}\bigl(y,\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},y,\mu)\bigr)\bigr\}\mathrm{d}\mu(y)=0,\\
&V(T,x_{0},x,\mu)=g(x_{0},x,\mu),\end{split}

for
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
has a classical solution
(V^{0},V)
in the sense that

1.

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto(\partial_{t}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu),\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu),\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
;

(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\mapsto(\partial_{\mu}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y),\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,y))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
;

2.

(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto(\partial_{t}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla^{2}_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
;

(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y)\mapsto(\partial_{\mu}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y),\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu,y))
is continuous on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,

3.

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}V^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)
and
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto(\nabla_{x_{0}}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu),\nabla_{x}V(t,x_{0},x,\mu))
are Lipschitz
continuous with respect to
(x_{0},\mu)
and
(x_{0},x,\mu)
respectively
(using the distance
{\mathbb{W}}_{1}
to handle the argument
\mu
), uniformly in
t
.

One solution is given by the master fields

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and

{\mathcal{U}}

introduced in
(
5.25
)
and
(
5.24
).
In particular,

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
have
first-order derivative in
t
and
second-order derivatives in
x_{0}
and these derivatives are jointly continuous (with respect to all their arguments).
Moreover, for any
t\in[0,T]
,
the mapping

x_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)
has
Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
\mu
, and the mapping
x_{0}\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)

has Hölder continuous second-order derivatives,
uniformly in
(x,\mu)
.

We use Propositions
5.15

and

5.16

to prove that
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and

{\mathcal{U}}
solve equations (
5.79
) and (
5.80
)
respectively.

We start with (
5.79
).
We claim that all the terms entering the time integrals in the right-hand side of
(
5.66
)
are Hölder continuous in the pair
(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0})
, uniformly in the other parameters.
For the term on the first line of
(
5.66
), this follows from the fact that, for all

t\in[0,T]
,

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to
{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(C,2)
for some
C\geq 0

(see Proposition
5.10
)
and that its derivatives are Hölder continuous in time (see
Proposition

5.12
).
For the second line of
(
5.66
),
the regularity of the term

\nabla_{y}\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},\mu,y)
follows from the same argument.
The regularity
of the term

\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(s,\tilde{X}_{s}^{0},y,\mu)
also follows from the fact that
{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
belongs to
{\mathscr{D}}(C,1,2)

for all
t\in[0,T]
,
and that its derivatives in
x
are Hölder continuous in time (see Lemma
5.4
).
The argument is the same for the
terms on
the third line of
(
5.66
).

Moreover, we observe that the drift of the SDE
(
5.67
) is also Hölder continuous in the arguments

(t,\tilde{X}_{t}^{0})
.

And then we can interpret
(
5.66
)
as the Kolmogorov formula for
representing
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)

as the solution to a second-order PDE, denoted
(E^{\mu})
, in the variables
(t_{0},x_{0})\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
This second-order PDE
(E^{\mu})
is driven by the generator of
(
5.67
). It has
g^{0}(\cdot,\mu)
as terminal condition and the remaining three terms
in the right-hand side of (
5.66
)
as source terms. By standard Schauder’s theory for uniformly parabolic PDEs with Hölder continuous coefficients, the PDE
(E^{\mu})
has a classical solution with second Hölder derivatives that are Hölder continuous
in time and space.
This says two things. First, for a fixed
\mu
,
(t_{0},x_{0})\mapsto{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t_{0},x_{0},\mu)
solves the
PDE
(E^{\mu})
.
By expanding all the terms, one easily recovers the master equation
(
5.79
).
In particular,
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
has a first-order derivative in time and
second-order derivatives in
x_{0}
.
Second, those derivatives are jointly continuous in
(t,x_{0},\mu)
(the proof is given a few lines below). In particular,

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
satisfies items 1 and 3 in the statement
(properties of the first order derivatives of
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
in
x_{0}
and
\mu

follow directly from Propositions

5.10

and

5.12
).
Joint regularity of
\partial_{t}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}

in
(t,x_{0},\mu)
follows
from Schauder’s theory again: the
(t,x_{0})
-Hölder norms
of
\partial_{t}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}

can be bounded independently of
\mu
; therefore, the latter two derivatives are locally compact
(in the space of Hölder functions) when
\mu
varies; using continuity of the coefficients
of

(E^{\mu})
with respect to the argument
\mu
, one easily deduces that

\partial_{t}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}
and
\nabla^{2}_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}

are indeed jointly continuous (using in particular the fact that

{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(C,2+({\mathbb{s}}-\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor)/2)
for some
C\geq 0
).

Thanks to Proposition
5.16
, one can proceed in a similar way to prove that

{\mathcal{U}}
solves the master equation
(
5.80
) and satisfies the properties
claimed in the statement.

∎

5.8
Back to the original coefficients

We now want to come back to the original system
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
).
To do this, we use the following lemma:

Lemma 5.18
.

Assume that

L^{0}
and

L

are as in
Assumption
(
A
)

and define the corresponding Hamiltonians

H^{0}
and
H
.
Then, for any given
R>0
, there exist two
coefficients
\hat{L}^{0}
and
\hat{H}
satisfying Assumption
(
Â
)
with respect to
a new constant
\kappa

and a function
\zeta
only depending on the parameters

\kappa
and
\lambda

in
(
A
)
, such that,
for any
(x,x_{0},p)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
with
|p|\leq R
,

(5.81)

\hat{H}(x,p)=H(x,p),\quad\hat{L}^{0}(x_{0},p)=L^{0}(x_{0},-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(x_{0},p)).

Without any loss of generality, we can assume
R>2
. We then introduce two auxiliary functions. We call
\zeta
a smooth cut-off function
from
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
to
[0,1]
such that
\zeta(p)=1
for
|p|\leq R
,

\zeta(p)=0
for
|p|\geq R^{2}
,
and

|\nabla_{p}\zeta(p)|\leq c/R^{2}

and

|\nabla_{pp}^{2}\zeta(p)|\leq c/R^{4}
for any
p
, where
c>0
is a universal constant.
We call
\varphi
an even smooth function from
{\mathbb{R}}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\varphi^{\prime\prime}
is non-decreasing on
[0,R^{2}]
and non-increasing
on
[R^{2},+\infty)
with

\varphi^{\prime\prime}(r)=1
for

r\in[0,R]
,

\varphi^{\prime\prime}(r)=r/R

for
r\in[2R,R^{2}]
,
and

\varphi^{\prime\prime}(r)=2R^{5}/r^{2}
for
r\geq 2R^{2}
. Assuming that
\varphi(0)=0
and
\varphi^{\prime}(0)=0
, we have

\varphi(r)=r^{2}/2
for
r\in[-R,R]
,

\varphi^{\prime}(r)\geq r^{2}/(2R)
for
r\in[2R,R^{2}]
and

\varphi^{\prime}(r)\in(0,4R^{3}]
for all
r\in{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}
. We then write
\Phi(p)
for
\varphi(|p|)
.
The function
\Phi
is smooth on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and

(5.82)

\nabla^{2}_{pp}\Phi(p)=\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(|p|)}{|p|}\Bigl[1-\frac{p}{|p|}\otimes\frac{p}{|p|}\Bigr]+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(|p|)\frac{p}{|p|}\otimes\frac{p}{|p|},\quad p\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}.

In particular,
\nabla^{2}_{pp}\Phi(p)=I_{d}
for
|p|\leq R
.
For
|p|\in[R,R^{2}]
,

\nabla^{2}_{pp}\Phi(p)\geq[|p|/(2R)]I_{d}

(in the sense of comparison of symmetric matrices) and,
for
|p|>R^{2}
,
\nabla^{2}_{pp}\Phi(p)
is positive definite.

With
\lambda^{\prime}
as in (
A5
), we consider

\hat{H}(x,p):=\Bigl(H(x,p)-\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{4}|p|^{2}\Bigr)\zeta(p)+\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}\Phi(p).

For
|p|\leq R
,

\hat{H}(x,p)=H(x,p)-\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{4}|p|^{2}+\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{4}|p|^{2}=H(x,p).

Moreover,

\begin{split}\nabla^{2}_{pp}\hat{H}(x,p)&=\Bigl(\nabla^{2}_{pp}H(x,p)-\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}I_{d}\Bigr)\zeta(p)+\Bigl(\nabla_{p}H(x,p)-\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}p\Bigr)\otimes\nabla\zeta(p)+\nabla\zeta(p)\otimes\Bigl(\nabla_{p}H(x,p)-\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}p\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\Bigl(H(x,p)-\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{4}|p|^{2}\Bigr)\nabla^{2}_{pp}\zeta(p)+\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}\nabla^{2}_{pp}\Phi(p).\end{split}

And then, using (
A5
), we know that
(in the sense of comparison of symmetric matrices)

\begin{split}&\nabla^{2}_{pp}\hat{H}(x,p)\geq-C\Bigl(|p|\times\frac{1}{R^{2}}+|p|^{2}\times\frac{1}{R^{4}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{|p|\in[R,R^{2}]\}}I_{d}+\tfrac{\lambda^{\prime}}{2}\nabla^{2}_{pp}\Phi(p),\end{split}

for a constant
C
only depending on the properties of
H
.
And then, by
(
5.82
), we get,
for
|p|\in[R,R^{2}]
,

\nabla^{2}_{pp}\hat{H}(x,p)\geq[\lambda^{\prime}|p|/(4R)-C(|p|/R^{2}+|p|^{2}/R^{4})]I_{d}=(|p|/R)[\lambda^{\prime}/4-C/R-C|p|/R^{3}]I_{d}.

For
R
large enough, the right-hand side is positive. And, then, it is straightforward to deduce that

\hat{H}
satisfies (
Â4
). Property
(
Â3
) follows from the fact that
\varphi
has bounded derivatives (of order greater than 1) and thus
\Phi
also has bounded derivatives (of order greater than 1).

As for the construction of
\hat{L}^{0}(x_{0},p)
, it suffices to consider

L^{0}(x_{0},-\zeta(p)\nabla_{p}H^{0}(x_{0},p))
.
∎

Proposition 5.19
.

Assume that

(f_{t}^{0})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,

(f_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
,

L^{0}
and

L

are as in
Assumptions
(
A1
)
,
(
A2
)
and
(
A4
)

and define the corresponding Hamiltonians

H^{0}
and
H
.
Consider
g^{0}
and
g
as in

(
Â2
)
, for a certain
{\mathbb{s}}\in(3,\lfloor\textrm{\rm\text{{s}}}\rfloor-(d/2+1)]
.
Given
R_{0}\geq 2{\mathfrak{L}}
(with
{\mathfrak{L}}
as in

(
Â2
)
), there exists a constant

{\mathfrak{C}}>0
, only depending on

d
,
\kappa
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\lambda
,
R_{0}
,
\sigma_{0}

and
\mathbb{s}
, such that, for
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
the system
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
)
with
\hat{H}
and
\hat{L}^{0}
given by Lemma

5.18

is uniquely solvable (in the sense of
Theorem

5.1
) and satisfies the
following conclusions for
{\mathbb{r}}:=(\lfloor{\mathbb{s}}\rfloor+{\mathbb{s}})/2

and for another constant
C
, only depending
on the parameters in
(
A
)
:

i.

For all
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}^{0}(C,{\mathbb{r}}-1)

and

{\mathcal{U}}(t,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}(C,{\mathbb{r}}-1,{\mathbb{s}})
;

ii.

With the same notation for the norm

\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{r}},{\mathbb{r}}-1}
as in footnote

2
,

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\to 0}\Big(\big\|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}}+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t+h,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},\cdot,\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r},\mathbb{r}-1}\Bigr)=0,\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\lim_{h\to 0}\Big(\big|\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu)-\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)\big|+\big\|\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t+h,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)-\delta_{\mu}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu,\cdot)\big\|_{\mathbb{r}-1}\Big)=0;\end{split}

iii.

The gradients

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)

and

(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)

are bounded by

R_{0}
. In particular, the solution to (
5.1
)–(
5.2
)
is also a solution to
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
) and any other solution to
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
) satisfying

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Z_{s}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{0}\|_{\rm BMO}\leq R_{0}

and

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\nabla_{x}u_{t}|\leq R_{0}

coincides with the unique
solution of
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
);

iv.

If the coefficients
f^{0}
,
f
,
g^{0}
and
g
satisfy the additional conditions of
Corollary

5.17
, then

({\mathcal{U}}^{0},{\mathcal{U}})
solves the
master equations
(
2.27
)–(
2.28
).

We let

{\mathbb{s}}:=(\lfloor\textrm{\rm\text{{s}}}\rfloor-(d/2+1)+3)/2
. For
R\geq R_{0}
and
T\leq{\mathfrak{C}}
,
with
{\mathfrak{C}}
depending on
d
,
{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\lambda
,
\kappa
,
R
,

\sigma_{0}
and

{\mathbb{s}}
, the system
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
)
with
\hat{H}
and
\hat{L}^{0}
given by Lemma

5.18
has a unique solution.
Item (i) follows from
Proposition

5.10
.
Item (ii) follows from
(
5.45
)
and
(
5.46
).

The main challenge is to prove item (iii).
We
first prove the bound for
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)
. To do so, we come back to the proof of the bound
(
5.4
). In fact, now that
{\bm{u}}
has been found,
we can regard the term
\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x))

as part of the source term in
(
5.2
), with an explicit bound (depending on
R
).
The idea is that the influence of the source term is very small as
t
gets close to
T
.
In particular, we can use any standard estimates for linear backward SPDEs, see for instance

[
14
, Lemma 4.3.7]
, to
a get a bound for
|\nabla_{x}u_{t}|
of the form
{\mathfrak{L}}+C_{R}(T-t)
, where

C_{R}
depends on
R
. Briefly,
{\mathfrak{L}}
comes from the boundary condition

g
and
C_{R}
comes from the source term. For
T-t
small enough,

C_{R}(T-t)
can be bounded by
{\mathfrak{L}}
. Equivalently, we can decrease the value of

{\mathfrak{C}}
so that
(t,x_{0},x,\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x}{\mathcal{U}}(t,x_{0},x,\mu)
is bounded by
R_{0}
.
The proof of the bound for

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}(t,x_{0},\mu)

is very similar, even though slightly more difficult.
The first step is to use the representation of

\nabla_{x_{0}}{\mathcal{U}}^{0}

in terms of
K^{0,x_{0}}
, see
Proposition
5.8
,
and then to return to the interpretation of the latter
in terms of
\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0}
,
see
(
5.34
).
The point is thus to come back to the equation
(
5.32
)
for
\delta{\bm{Y}}^{0}
.
At this stage, we already have a bound for

\delta{\bm{X}}^{0}
and

\delta{\bm{Z}}^{0}
. Therefore, we can also obtain a bound
for
|\delta{Y}^{0}_{t}|
of the form

{\mathfrak{L}}+C_{R}(T-t)

and then
complete the proof as done for

|\nabla_{x}{\bm{u}}|
.

We now turn to the rest of the claim (iii).
The fact that the solution to
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
)
is also a solution to
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
)
is easily checked and follows
from
(
5.81
) (by the way,
the argument is similar for item (iv)).
It thus remains
to prove that uniqueness to
(
2.11
)–(
2.12
)
holds true within the class of
solutions satisfying

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Z_{s}^{0}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{0}\|_{\rm BMO}\leq R_{0}

(which condition is obviously satisfied by the solution
to
(
5.1
)–(
5.2
)
since
T\leq 1
)
and

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\nabla_{x}u_{t}|\leq R_{0}
.
Under the latter bound, we can replace
H(x,\nabla_{x}u_{t})

by
\hat{H}(x,\nabla_{x}u_{t})
in
the system (
2.12
).
This makes it possible to identify
the two systems (
2.12
)
and
(
5.2
) (i.e., the systems for the minor player). The difficulty
comes from the fact that
we cannot do the same for the major player and identify the systems
(
2.11
)
and
(
5.1
): indeed, we cannot replace
L^{0}(X_{t}^{0},-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0}))

by

\hat{L}^{0}(X_{t}^{0},Z_{t}^{0})
because the
BMO bound on
{\bm{Z}}^{0}

is too weak to do so. To overcome this issue,
we must revisit the proof of
Theorem

5.1

with

\hat{L}^{0}(x_{0},p)
being replaced by

L^{0}(x_{0},-\nabla_{p}H^{0}(x_{0},p))
(but with the same
\hat{H}
as therein):
since the difficulty only comes from the new ‘major’ system
(
5.1
),
the only point is to prove that
(
5.11
)
remains true
in this new setting with the additional
assumption that
\tilde{\bm{X}}^{0,i}={\bm{X}}^{0,i}
for

i=1,2
(since we are dealing with uniqueness,
we can restrict the analysis to fixed points
of the mapping
{\mathfrak{T}}
,
with the latter being
defined right below
(
5.8
)).
Here is the way we get the analogue of
(
5.11
). Removing the ‘tilde’ in
the second step of
the proof of Theorem

5.1
, we first observe
from the Lipschitz property of

\nabla_{p}H^{0}
and Gronwall’s lemma that

(5.83)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}|\leq C\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{t}^{0,1}-Z_{t}^{0,2}|\mathrm{d}t,

for a constant
C

only
depending
on
d
,
\kappa
,

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\lambda
,
\sigma_{0}
and
{\mathbb{s}}
, where here and below we assume without any loss of generality that
T\leq 1
(equivalently
{\mathfrak{C}}\leq 1
).
As for the
backward equation,
we
notice that there exist two progressively measurable processes
(\theta_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
and

(\vartheta_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
, with values in

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, such that
|\theta_{t}|\leq C(1+|Z_{t}^{0,1}|+|Z_{t}^{0,2}|)
and
|\vartheta_{t}|\leq C
for all
t\in[0,T]
and a
possibly new value of
C
, such that

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\bigl(Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}\bigr)&=-\Bigl[f_{t}(X_{t}^{0,1},\mu_{t}^{1})-f_{t}(X_{t}^{0,2},\mu_{t}^{2})+\vartheta_{t}\cdot\bigl(X_{t}^{0,1}-X_{t}^{0,2}\bigr)\Bigr]\mathrm{d}t\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\theta_{t}\cdot\bigl(Z_{t}^{0,1}-Z_{t}^{0,2}\bigr)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma_{0}\bigl(Z_{t}^{0,1}-Z_{t}^{0,2}\bigr)\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\quad t\in[0,T].\end{split}

We then let

{\mathcal{E}}_{t}:={\mathcal{E}}_{t}\biggl(\sigma_{0}^{-1}\int_{0}^{\cdot}\theta_{s}\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{0}\biggr),\quad t\in[0,T].

And then, by the BMO properties of
{\bm{Z}}^{0,1}
and
{\bm{Z}}^{0,2}
,
we can apply Girsanov theorem and then obtain

\begin{split}Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}&={\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl\{{\mathcal{E}}_{T}\biggl[g^{0}(X_{T}^{0,1},\mu_{T}^{1})-g^{0}(X_{T}^{0,2},\mu_{T}^{2})+\int_{t}^{T}\bigl[f^{0}_{s}(X_{s}^{0,1},\mu_{s}^{1})-f^{0}_{s}(X_{s}^{0,2},\mu_{s}^{2})\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t}^{T}\vartheta_{s}\cdot\bigl(X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}\bigr)\mathrm{d}s\biggr]\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr\},\quad t\in[0,T].\end{split}

In fact, the BMO norm of
{\bm{\theta}}
is bounded by a known constant. We deduce from

[
40
, Theorem 3.1]

that there exist two conjugate exponents
p,q>1
, only depending on

d
,
\kappa
,

{\mathfrak{L}}
,
\lambda
,
\sigma_{0}
and
{\mathbb{s}}
, such that

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\Bigl({\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{-1}{\mathcal{E}}_{T}\Bigr)^{q}|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\Bigr]\leq C,

the value of
C
being allowed to vary from line to line.
Then, by Hölder inequality,

(5.84)

\begin{split}|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}|^{p}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]^{1/p}\\
&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}|^{2p}+\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{2p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]^{1/(2p)}.\end{split}

Also, by squaring the difference

{\bm{Y}}^{0,1}-{\bm{Y}}^{0,2}
(in (
2.11
)),
we obtain the following variant of
[
55
, Proposition 2.2]
:

{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely,
for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\begin{split}&|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{T}|Z_{s}^{0,1}-Z_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{2}+\sup_{s\in[t,T]}|Y_{s}^{0,1}-Y_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}\int_{t}^{T}\bigl(1+|Z_{s}^{0,1}|+|Z_{s}^{0,2}|\bigr)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr].\end{split}

Here, using again the BMO property together with
[
40
, Theorem 2.2]
, we can assume without any loss of generality that

{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{t}^{T}\bigl(1+|Z_{s}^{0,1}|+|Z_{s}^{0,2}|\bigr)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\biggr)^{q}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\leq C.

And then, by a new application of Hölder inequality (assuming without any loss of generality that
p\in(2,+\infty)
and
q\in(1,2)
),

\begin{split}&|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{T}|Z_{s}^{0,1}-Z_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{2}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]+C{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{s\in[t,T]}|Y_{s}^{0,1}-Y_{s}^{0,2}|^{2p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]^{1/p}.\end{split}

Inserting (
5.84
) and using Doob’s inequality, we get

\begin{split}&|Y_{t}^{0,1}-Y_{t}^{0,2}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{T}|Z_{s}^{0,1}-Z_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\leq C{\mathbb{E}}\biggl[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}|^{2p}+\sup_{s\in[0,T]}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{2p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]^{1/p}.\end{split}

Taking the power
p
and then the supremum over
t\in[0,T]
, we get

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\int_{t}^{T}|Z_{s}^{0,1}-Z_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{0}\biggr]^{p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}\biggr]\\
&\leq CT^{p}{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\biggl[\biggl(\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{s}^{0,1}-Z_{s}^{0,2}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\biggr)^{p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}\biggr]+C{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{2p}\,|\,{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}\Bigr],\end{split}

with the second line following from
(
5.83
).
Bounding from below the left-hand side by
the value at
t=0
,
we get
(for a possibly new value of
{\mathfrak{C}}\leq 1
):

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|X_{s}^{0,1}-X_{s}^{0,2}|^{2p}\Bigr]\leq CT{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}{\mathbb{W}}_{1}(\mu_{s}^{1},\mu_{s}^{2})^{2p}\Bigr].\end{split}

The fact that we use here

2p
as exponent (and not
2
) makes a difference with
(
5.11
). However, we can easily complete the proof of uniqueness
by inserting the above bound in
(
5.14
) (with a possibly new value of
p
).
∎

6
Appendix

6.1
Convenient form of the chain rule over

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

In this subsection, we consider
two filtered probability spaces

(\Omega^{0},{\mathcal{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{F}}^{0},{\mathbb{P}}^{0})

and

(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{F}},{{\mathbb{P}}})
equipped with two
Brownian motions
(B_{t}^{0})_{0\leq t\leq T}

and
(B_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
with values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
We are also given two
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
-valued
Itô processes

\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{0}=b_{t}^{0}\mathrm{d}t+\varsigma_{t}^{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0},\\
&\mathrm{d}X_{t}=b_{t}\mathrm{d}t+\varsigma_{t}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\quad t\geq 0,\end{split}

with square-integrable conditions
X_{0}^{0}
and
X_{0}
, respectively

{\mathcal{F}}_{0}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
-measurable.
The processes
(b_{t}^{0})_{t\geq 0}
and
(\varsigma_{t}^{0})_{t\geq 0}

are constructed on
\Omega^{0}
and are
{\mathbb{F}}^{0}
-progressively measurable
(with values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\otimes{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
respectively), and the processes

(b_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and
(\varsigma_{t})_{t\geq 0}

are constructed on

\Omega^{0}\times\Omega

and are

{\mathbb{F}}^{0}\otimes{\mathbb{F}}
-progressively measurable
(also with values in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\otimes{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
respectively).

We also assume that, for any
T>0
, the processes

(\varsigma_{t}^{0})_{0\leq t\leq T}
and

(\varsigma_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}

are bounded by a deterministic constant. And, we assume that

{\mathbb{E}}^{0}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|b_{t}^{0}|^{2}\Bigr]+{\mathbb{E}}^{0}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|b_{t}|^{2}\Bigr]<\infty.

Lastly,
for any
t\geq 0
,
we let

\mu_{t}(\omega^{0}):={\mathcal{L}}^{0}(X_{t})(\omega^{0}),\quad\omega^{0}\in\Omega^{0},

when
(X_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is regarded as a random variable with values in

{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
. Equivalently,

\mu_{t}(\omega^{0})
is seen as an element of

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\ell:[0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\ni(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\ell(t,x_{0},\mu)\in{\mathbb{R}}
be differentiable with respect to
t
,

x_{0}
and
\mu
such that

1.

The functions
(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\partial_{t}\ell(t,x_{0},\mu)
and

(t,x_{0},\mu)\mapsto\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(t,x_{0},\mu)

are jointly continuous (with respect to
{\mathbb{W}}_{1}
in the argument
\mu
);

2.

The function
[0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\mapsto\partial_{\mu}\ell(t,x_{0},\mu,y)

is jointly continuous (with respect to
{\mathbb{W}}_{1}
in the argument
\mu
)
and is differentiable with respect to
y
,
the derivative

[0,+\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(t,x_{0},\mu,y)\mapsto\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}\ell(t,x_{0},\mu,y)

being also jointly continuous.

Then,
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-almost surely, for any
t\geq 0
,

\begin{split}\mathrm{d}_{t}\bigl[\ell\bigl(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t}\bigr)\bigr]&=\biggl[\partial_{t}\ell\bigl(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t}\bigr)+{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[ b_{t}\cdot\partial_{\mu}\ell(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t},X_{t})\bigr]+\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[{\rm Tr}\bigl( \varsigma_{t}\varsigma_{t}^{\dagger}\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}\ell(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t},X_{t})\bigr)\bigr]\\
&\hskip 5.0pt+b_{t}^{0}\cdot\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})+\tfrac{1}{2}{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\varsigma_{t}^{0}\bigl(\varsigma_{t}^{0}\bigr)^{\dagger}\nabla_{x_{0}}^{2}\ell(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\Bigr)\biggr]\mathrm{d}t+\bigl(\varsigma_{t}^{0}\bigr)^{\dagger}\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t})\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{t}^{0}.\end{split}

Fix
T>0
. It suffices to prove the formula for
t\in[0,T]
.
For a mesh
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}=t
of the interval
[0,t]
, we
have

\begin{split}&\ell\bigl(t,X_{t}^{0},\mu_{t}\bigr)-\ell\bigl(0,X_{0}^{0},\mu_{0}\bigr)\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigl[\bigl\{\ell\bigl(t_{i},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)-\ell\bigl(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}}\bigr)\bigr\}+\bigl\{\ell\bigl(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}}\bigr)-\ell\bigl(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i-1}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}}\bigr)\bigr\}\Bigr].\end{split}

By freezing
\omega^{0}
, we can expand

\ell(t_{i},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}})-\ell(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}})
by means of the standard chain rule
on
[0,+\infty)\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, see
[
19
, Theorem 5.99]
.
For
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
,
we obtain

\begin{split}&\ell\bigl(t_{i},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i}}\bigr)-\ell\bigl(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}}\bigr)\\
&=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\partial_{t}\ell\bigl(s,X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{s}\bigr)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[ b_{s}\cdot\partial_{\mu}\ell(s,X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{s},X_{s})\bigr]\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[{\rm Tr}\bigl( \varsigma_{s}\varsigma_{s}^{\dagger}\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}\ell(s,X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{s},X_{s})\bigr)\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\partial_{t}\ell\bigl(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s}\bigr)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[ b_{s}\cdot\partial_{\mu}\ell(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s},X_{s})\bigr]\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[{\rm Tr}\bigl( \varsigma_{s}\varsigma_{s}^{\dagger}\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}\ell(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s},X_{s})\bigr)\bigr]\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\varpi^{0}_{t_{i-1},t_{i}},\end{split}

where
(\varpi^{0}_{r,s})_{0\leq r\leq s\leq T}
is a collection of
{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{T}
-measurable random variables satisfying

\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\varpi^{0}_{t_{i-1},t_{i}}|\rightarrow 0
in
{\mathbb{P}}^{0}
-probability as

n
tends to
\infty
(and the step size of the mesh tends to
0
).
The derivation of the above identity relies on the fact that the
path
(X^{0}_{s})_{0\leq s\leq T}

is continuous and the derivatives

(s,\xi,\mu,y)\mapsto\partial_{\mu}\ell(s,\xi,\mu,y)

and

(s,\xi,\mu,y)\mapsto\nabla_{y}\partial_{\mu}\ell(s,\xi,\mu,y)

are continuous (and thus) bounded on (compact) sets of the form

[0,T]\times\{\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:|\xi|\leq a\}\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, for any
a>0

(and similarly for the derivative

\partial_{t}\ell
).
Moreover, by standard Itô formula, we have in a similar manner:

\begin{split}&\ell\bigl(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}}\bigr)-\ell\bigl(t_{i-1},X_{t_{i-1}}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}}\bigr)\\
&=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}b_{s}^{0}\cdot\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(t_{i-1},X_{s}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}})\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\varsigma_{s}^{0}\bigl(\varsigma_{s}^{0}\bigr)^{\dagger}\nabla_{x_{0}}^{2}\ell(t_{i-1},X_{s}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\bigl(\varsigma_{s}^{0}\bigr)^{\dagger}\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(t_{i-1},X_{s}^{0},\mu_{t_{i-1}})\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{0}\\
&=\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}b_{s}^{0}\cdot\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\varsigma_{s}^{0}\bigl(\varsigma_{s}^{0}\bigr)^{\dagger}\nabla_{x_{0}}^{2}\ell(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})\Bigr)\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{i-1}}^{t_{i}}\bigl(\varsigma_{s}^{0}\bigr)^{\dagger}\nabla_{x_{0}}\ell(s,X_{s}^{0},\mu_{s})\cdot\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{0}+\varpi^{0}_{t_{i-1},t_{i}},\end{split}

for a possible new choice of
(\varpi^{0}_{r,s})_{0\leq r<s\leq T}
.
The above identity follows from the continuity of the path

(\mu_{s})_{0\leq s\leq T}
(with respect to
{\mathbb{W}}_{1}
).

Combining the last two displays, summing over
i
and letting
the step size of the mesh tend to
0
, we complete the proof.
∎

6.2
Estimates for transport-diffusion equations

We here collect several results regarding the long-time behaviour of transport-diffusion equation on the torus.
We start with the following first lemma:

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
T>0
,
\mathbb{r}\geq 1
and
b:[0,\infty)\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be a measurable function such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|b(t,\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{r}-1}<\infty
.
Then,
there exist two constants
C
and
\gamma>0
, depending on the quantity

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|b(t,\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{r}-1}

but not on
T
, such that
the solution to the transport-diffusion equation
(set
on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta\varphi_{t}+b(t,\cdot)\cdot\nabla\varphi_{t}=0,\quad t\in[0,T];\quad\varphi_{T}=\phi,\end{split}

for
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{r}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
satisfies

(6.1)

\|\varphi_{t}-\bar{\varphi}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{r}}\leq C\exp\bigl(-\gamma(T-t)\bigr)\|\phi\|_{\mathbb{r}},

where

\bar{\varphi}_{t}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\varphi_{t}(x)\mathrm{d}x
.

Notice that the result is standard for
t
close to
T
(say
T-t\leq 1
).
It just provides a control of the
{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{r}}
-norm of the solution
in term of the
{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{r}}
-norm of the terminal condition
and the
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathbb{r}}-1}
-norm of the velocity field.

In order to get the exponential decay when
t
gets away from
T
, we recall from
[
11
, Lemma 7.4]
that

(6.2)

\|\varphi_{t}-\bar{\varphi}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\exp\bigl(-\gamma(T-t)\bigr)\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad t\in[0,T],

with
C
and
\gamma
as in the statement.
Take now
\delta\in(0,1)
. For
t\in[0,T-\delta]
, we write

\varphi_{t}=P_{\delta}\varphi_{t+\delta}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}P_{r-t}\bigl[b(r,\cdot)\cdot\nabla\varphi_{r}\bigr]\mathrm{d}r,

where
(P_{s})_{s\geq 0}
stands here for the standard heat kernel on the torus

{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, namely
(P_{s})_{s\geq 0}
is the semi-group generated by the
operator

\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta
.
And then,

\varphi_{t}-\bar{\varphi}_{t+\delta}=P_{\delta}\bigl(\varphi_{t+\delta}-\bar{\varphi}_{t+\delta}\bigr)+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}P_{r-t}\bigl[b(r,\cdot)\cdot\nabla\varphi_{r}\bigr]\mathrm{d}r.

In particular, for any
integer
k\in\{0,\cdots,\lfloor\mathbb{r}\rfloor-1\}
and any real
\eta\in(0,1)
,

\|\nabla^{k+1}\varphi_{t}\|_{\eta}=\bigl\|\nabla^{k+1}\bigl(\varphi_{t}-\bar{\varphi}_{t+\delta}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\eta}\leq C_{\delta}\|\varphi_{t+\delta}-\bar{\varphi}_{t+\delta}\|_{k}+C\int_{t}^{t+\delta}(r-t)^{-(1+\eta)/2}\|b(r,\cdot)\cdot\nabla\varphi_{r}\|_{k}\mathrm{d}r,

where
C_{\delta}
depends on
\delta
.

Assuming that
(
6.2
) holds true with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{k}
instead of

\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{r}}
, we can bound

\|b(r,\cdot)\cdot\nabla\varphi_{r}\|_{k}=\|b(r,\cdot)\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{r}-\bar{\varphi}_{r})\|_{k}
by
C_{k}(\exp(-\gamma(T-r))\|\phi\|_{\mathbb{r}}+\|\nabla^{k+1}\varphi_{r}\|_{\eta})
. We obtain

\begin{split}\|\nabla^{k+1}\varphi_{t}\|_{\eta}&\leq C_{k,\delta}\exp\bigl(-\gamma(T-t)\bigr)\|\phi\|_{\mathbb{r}}+C_{k}\int_{t}^{t+\delta}(r-t)^{-(1+\eta)/2}\|\nabla^{k+1}\varphi_{r}\|_{\eta}\mathrm{d}r,\end{split}

and then, for
t+\delta\leq T
,

\exp\bigl(\gamma(T-t)\bigr)\,\|\nabla^{k+1}\varphi_{t}\|_{\eta}\leq C_{k,\delta}\|\phi\|_{\mathbb{r}}+C_{k}\delta^{(1-\eta)/{2}}\exp\bigl(\gamma\delta\bigr)\sup_{t\leq r\leq t+\delta}\Bigl[\exp\bigl(\gamma(T-r)\bigr)\|\nabla^{k+1}\varphi_{r}\|_{\eta}\Bigr].

Choosing
\delta
small enough and then taking the supremum over
t\leq T-\delta
, we get a bound for the left-hand side.
We then get
the result by iterating on the value of
k
.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
T>0
,
\mathbb{r}\geq 1
and
b:[0,\infty)\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be a measurable function such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|b(t,\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{r}}<\infty
.
Then,
there exist two constants
C
and
\gamma>0
, depending on

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|b(t,\cdot)\|_{\mathbb{r}}<\infty

but not on
T
, such that
the solution to the conservation equation
(set on
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
)

(6.3)

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}q_{t}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Delta q_{t}+\textrm{\rm div}_{x}\bigl(b(t,\cdot)q_{t}\bigr)=0,\quad t\in[0,T];\quad q_{0}=q,\end{split}

for a smooth initial condition
q:{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

with
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}q(x)\mathrm{d}x=0
,
satisfies

\|q_{t}\|_{-\mathbb{r}}\leq C\exp\bigl(-\gamma t\bigr)\|q\|_{-\mathbb{r}},\quad t\in[0,T].

When

\bar{q}:=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}q(x)\mathrm{d}x\not=0
,
we deduce from the conservative structure that

\|q_{t}-\bar{q}\|_{-\mathbb{r}}\leq C\|q-\bar{q}\|_{-\mathbb{r}},\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}q_{t}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\bar{q},\quad t\in[0,T].

The proof is done by duality. For
\phi
and
(\varphi_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
as in the statement of Lemma

6.2
, we compute
(by decomposing
q
in positive and negative parts, it suffices to derive the identity below
when
q
is a probability measure, in which case the result follows on Itô-Krylov formula
for Itô processes with a non-degenerate diffusion coefficient and abounded drift)

\mathrm{d}_{t}\bigl(\varphi_{t},q_{t}\bigr)=0,\quad t\in[0,T],

where
(\cdot,\cdot)
is here understood as the duality bracket between
{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{r}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
and

{\mathcal{C}}^{-\mathbb{r}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
so that

(\phi,q_{T})=(\varphi_{0},q)\leq C\exp\bigl(-\gamma T\bigr)\|q\|_{-\mathbb{r}}\|\phi\|_{\mathbb{r}}.

The result follows by maximizing over
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbb{r}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
satisfying

\|\phi\|_{\mathbb{r}}\leq 1
.
∎
